```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ved-ava adalah dewi air dan kesuburan bangsa-bangsa Fino-Ugria, terutama Mordvin, digambarkan mirip putri duyung dan dihormati para nelayan.",
    "en": "Ved-ava is a water and fertility goddess of the Finno-Ugric peoples, especially the Mordvins, pictured like a mermaid and honoured by fishermen.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ved-ava-c01",
      "ved-ava-c02",
      "ved-ava-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ved-ava, juga disebut Vedmastor-ava (\"ibu air\") atau Ved-azěrava (\"nyonya air\"), adalah dewi air dan kesuburan yang dikenal beberapa bangsa Fino-Ugria yang hidup dari menangkap ikan. Bagi orang Mordvin ia Ibu Air, penguasa perairan dan hasilnya; dalam mitologi Finlandia, Estonia, dan Karelia ia dikenal dengan nama seperti Veden Emo, Veteema, dan Veden Emä.",
      "en": "Ved-ava, whose other names include Vedmastor-ava, the water-mother, and Ved-azěrava, the water-hostess, is a goddess of water and fertility known among several fishing Finno-Ugric peoples. To the Mordvins she was the Water Mother who governed the waters and all they yield; in Finnish, Estonian and Karelian mythology she is known by names such as Veden Emo, Veteema and Veden Emä.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ved-ava-c01",
        "ved-ava-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia biasanya digambarkan mirip putri duyung, berambut panjang dan berekor ikan, kadang bernyanyi untuk memikat manusia. Para nelayan mempersembahkan tangkapan pertama kepadanya dan mematuhi pantangan saat menangkap ikan. Semula ia terutama dewi kesuburan, pelindung pernikahan, persalinan, dan cinta, dan kemandulan dianggap akibat kemarahannya; wujud putri duyung dan kaitannya dengan orang tenggelam muncul belakangan.",
      "en": "She is usually pictured like a mermaid, long-haired and fish-tailed, sometimes singing to seduce people. Fishermen offered her the first of their catch and kept taboos while fishing. Originally she was mainly a fertility goddess, protector of marriage, childbirth and love, and infertility was blamed on her anger; her mermaid form and link with drowning came later.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ved-ava-c02",
        "ved-ava-c03",
        "ved-ava-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut takhayul pelaut, melihatnya membawa penyakit dan skorbut, dan orang yang melihat Vediava sering jatuh sakit atau tenggelam.",
      "en": "A sailors' superstition held that seeing her brought illness and scurvy, and a person who sees Vediava often falls ill or drowns.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ved-ava-c06",
        "ved-ava-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Bagi bangsa Fino-Ugria nelayan, Ved-ava mengatur hasil perairan; persembahan tangkapan pertama dan pantangan saat menangkap ikan dilakukan untuknya.",
    "en": "For fishing Finno-Ugric peoples Ved-ava governed the bounty of the waters; the first catch was offered and taboos kept for her.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c01",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s1",
      "quote": "Ved-ava, also called Vedmastor-ava (water-mother) or Ved-azěrava (water-hostess) is a water and fertility deity, common to several Finno-Ugric peoples traditionally dependent on fishing. Among the Mordvins (an area in what is now Western Russia) she was the Water Mother, ruler of the waters and their bounty, as well as an important fertility deity.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ved-ava, juga disebut Vedmastor-ava (\"ibu air\") atau Ved-azěrava (\"nyonya air\"), adalah dewi air dan kesuburan yang dikenal beberapa bangsa Fino-Ugria yang hidup dari menangkap ikan; bagi orang Mordvin (wilayah yang kini Rusia barat) ia Ibu Air, penguasa perairan dan hasilnya, sekaligus dewi kesuburan penting.",
        "en": "Ved-ava, also called Vedmastor-ava (\"water-mother\") or Ved-azěrava (\"water-hostess\"), is a water and fertility deity shared by several fishing Finno-Ugric peoples; among the Mordvins (in what is now western Russia) she was the Water Mother, ruler of the waters and their bounty and an important fertility deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c02",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s1",
      "quote": "She is generally depicted as a water creature resembling a mermaid, with long hair, large breasts, and the lower body of a fish complete with tail, and is sometimes said to play or sing, seducing humans with her music.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia umumnya digambarkan sebagai makhluk air mirip putri duyung berambut panjang, berpayudara besar, dan bertubuh bawah ikan berekor, yang kadang memainkan musik atau bernyanyi untuk memikat manusia.",
        "en": "She is generally depicted as a mermaid-like water creature with long hair, large breasts and a fish's lower body with tail, sometimes playing music or singing to seduce people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c03",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s1",
      "quote": "Fishermen sacrificed to her the first of their catch and observed numerous taboos related to her while fishing. She was originally mainly a fertility goddess, with her form as a mermaid and association with drowning coming later.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para nelayan mempersembahkan tangkapan pertama kepadanya dan mematuhi banyak pantangan terkait dirinya saat menangkap ikan; semula ia terutama dewi kesuburan, sedangkan wujud putri duyung dan kaitannya dengan orang tenggelam muncul belakangan.",
        "en": "Fishermen offered her the first of their catch and kept many taboos about her while fishing; she was originally mainly a fertility goddess, her mermaid form and link with drowning coming later."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c04",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s1",
      "quote": "In Mordvin tradition, she was the protector of marriage, childbirth, love, and fertility. Young couples hoping to have children would pray to her, and infertility was often ascribed to her being angry with whomever was suffering it.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Mordvin ia pelindung pernikahan, persalinan, cinta, dan kesuburan; pasangan muda yang ingin punya anak berdoa kepadanya, dan kemandulan sering dianggap akibat kemarahannya.",
        "en": "In Mordvin tradition she protected marriage, childbirth, love and fertility; young couples hoping for children prayed to her, and infertility was often blamed on her anger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c05",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s2",
      "quote": "Ved-ava (conosciuta come Veden Emo oppure Veen Emo nella mitologia finlandese, Veteema nella mitologia estone e Wedhen Eme oppure Veden Emä in quella careliana è considerata una divinità delle acque.",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ved-ava, yang dikenal sebagai Veden Emo atau Veen Emo dalam mitologi Finlandia, Veteema dalam mitologi Estonia, dan Wedhen Eme atau Veden Emä dalam mitologi Karelia, dianggap dewi perairan.",
        "en": "Ved-ava, known as Veden Emo or Veen Emo in Finnish mythology, Veteema in Estonian and Wedhen Eme or Veden Emä in Karelian, is considered a goddess of the waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c06",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s2",
      "quote": "Una superstizione dei marinari diceva che vederla portava malattie e scorbuto.",
      "locator": "Tradizione orale",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut takhayul pelaut, melihatnya membawa penyakit dan sariawan (skorbut).",
        "en": "A sailors' superstition said that seeing her brought illness and scurvy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ved-ava-c07",
      "source_id": "ved-ava-s3",
      "quote": "Pamačius Vediavą žmogus suserga, dažnai paskęsta. Šios dievybės dažnai siejamos su skendimu.",
      "locator": "įvadas",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang yang melihat Vediava jatuh sakit dan sering tenggelam; dewi-dewi ini kerap dikaitkan dengan orang tenggelam.",
        "en": "A person who sees Vediava falls ill and often drowns; these deities are often associated with drowning."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Zheuzhyk adalah penjaga sungai dan danau dalam mitologi Belarus, lelaki tua kurus berjanggut merah yang menolong orang dalam kesulitan di air.",
    "en": "Zheuzhyk is the guardian of rivers and lakes in Belarusian mythology, a thin red-bearded old man who rescues people in trouble on the water.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "zheuzhyk-c01",
      "zheuzhyk-c02",
      "zheuzhyk-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Zheuzhyk adalah makhluk baik dalam mitologi Belarus yang tinggal jauh di dasar danau dan dianggap penjaga sungai dan danau di Belarus. Ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua kurus berjanggut merah panjang dengan lengan dan kaki panjang. Pada siang hari ia berenang di bawah air, dan pada malam hari mengelilingi wilayahnya dengan perahu sambil membelah ombak.",
      "en": "Zheuzhyk is a good being of Belarusian mythology living deep in the lake bed and regarded as the guardian of the rivers and lakes of Belarus. He is pictured as a lean old man with a long red beard and long limbs. By day he swims underwater, and by night he rounds his domain by boat, parting the waves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "zheuzhyk-c01",
        "zheuzhyk-c02",
        "zheuzhyk-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dipercaya bahwa Zheuzhyk pasti datang menolong orang yang mendapat kesulitan di air, dan ia cukup kuat untuk menghentikan angin dan badai. Semua roh air, termasuk Vadavik dan Lazavik, tunduk kepadanya, dan namanya muncul dalam peribahasa tentang kecekatan. Namun sebagai dewa air Belarus kuno, ia digambarkan dan kemungkinan dikarang oleh folkloris Pavel Shpilevsky.",
      "en": "Zheuzhyk is believed to come without fail to rescue anyone in trouble on the water, and is strong enough to stop wind and storm. All water spirits, including the Vadavik and Lazavik, obey him, and his name appears in sayings about nimbleness. As an ancient Belarusian water god, however, he was described and probably invented by the folklorist Pavel Shpilevsky.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "zheuzhyk-c03",
        "zheuzhyk-c04",
        "zheuzhyk-c05",
        "zheuzhyk-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "url": "https://be.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%96%D1%8D%D1%9E%D0%B6%D1%8B%D0%BA_(%D0%BF%D0%B5%D1%80%D1%81%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B0%D0%B6)",
      "title": "Жэўжык (персанаж)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (be)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "be",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c01",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s1",
      "quote": "is a creature of Belarusian mythology. It is considered to be a good creature that lives deeply in the lake bed. Zheuzhyk is considered to be the guardian of rivers and lakes of Belarus.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zheuzhyk adalah makhluk mitologi Belarus yang baik hati, tinggal jauh di dasar danau, dan dianggap penjaga sungai dan danau di Belarus.",
        "en": "Zheuzhyk is a creature of Belarusian mythology, a good being living deep in the lake bed and regarded as the guardian of the rivers and lakes of Belarus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c02",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s1",
      "quote": "In Belarusian folklore Zheuzhyk is described as a thin old man with a long red beard, long and thin arms and legs.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam folklor Belarus, Zheuzhyk digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua kurus berjanggut merah panjang dengan lengan dan kaki panjang dan kurus.",
        "en": "In Belarusian folklore Zheuzhyk is described as a thin old man with a long red beard and long, thin arms and legs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c03",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s1",
      "quote": "As it is said in Belarusian myths, during a day Zheuzhyk swims underwater, and at night Zheuzhyk slowly swims round its possession on boat, dispersing the waves. There is a belief, that is somebody gets into a trouble on water, Zheuzhyk will surely come to rescue. Zheuzhyk is so strong that it may even stop the wind and the storm.",
      "locator": "Life",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada siang hari Zheuzhyk berenang di bawah air dan pada malam hari mengelilingi wilayahnya dengan perahu sambil membelah ombak; dipercaya bila seseorang mendapat kesulitan di air, Zheuzhyk pasti datang menolong, dan ia begitu kuat hingga bisa menghentikan angin dan badai.",
        "en": "By day Zheuzhyk swims underwater, and by night slowly rounds his domain by boat, parting the waves; it is believed that if someone gets into trouble on the water Zheuzhyk will surely come to the rescue, and he is so strong he can even stop wind and storm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c04",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s2",
      "quote": "Как древнебелорусский покровитель рек или водяное божество описан (и, вероятно, придуман) фольклористом Павлом Шпилевским (Древлянским).",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai pelindung sungai atau dewa air Belarus kuno, Zheuzhyk digambarkan, dan kemungkinan dikarang, oleh folkloris Pavel Shpilevsky (Drevlyansky).",
        "en": "As an ancient Belarusian patron of rivers or water deity, Zheuzhyk was described, and probably invented, by the folklorist Pavel Shpilevsky (Drevlyansky)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c05",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s3",
      "quote": "Жэўжыку падпарадкоўваюцца ўсе вадзяныя духі, у тым ліку Вадавік і Лазавік.",
      "locator": "Лад жыцця",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Semua roh air tunduk kepada Zheuzhyk, termasuk Vadavik dan Lazavik.",
        "en": "All water spirits obey Zheuzhyk, including the Vadavik and Lazavik."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c06",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s3",
      "quote": "«Які Жэўжык» — які спрытны! «Бы Жэўжык нырае» — нібы Жэўжык плавае;",
      "locator": "Лад жыцця",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam peribahasa Belarus, \"Seperti Zheuzhyk!\" berarti betapa cekatan, dan \"Menyelam seperti Zheuzhyk\" berarti berenang seperti Zheuzhyk.",
        "en": "In Belarusian sayings, \"What a Zheuzhyk!\" means how nimble, and \"Dives like Zheuzhyk\" means swims like Zheuzhyk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zheuzhyk-c07",
      "source_id": "zheuzhyk-s1",
      "quote": "Sculpture of Zheuzhyk, by Belarusian sculptor Anton Shipitsa on the basis of illustrations of Valery Slauk",
      "locator": "image caption",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terdapat patung Zheuzhyk karya pematung Belarus Anton Shipitsa berdasarkan ilustrasi Valery Slauk.",
        "en": "There is a sculpture of Zheuzhyk by the Belarusian sculptor Anton Shipitsa based on illustrations by Valery Slauk."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
```

```json
    "id": "Zhytsen adalah roh ladang gandum hitam dalam mitologi Belarus, kakek kecil bermata tiga yang menjaga panen dan memindahkan bulir yang tertinggal ke ladang petani rajin.",
    "en": "Zhytsen is the rye-field spirit of Belarusian mythology, a little three-eyed old man who guards the harvest and moves leftover grain to diligent farmers' fields.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Zhytsen adalah makhluk baik dalam mitologi Belarus yang tinggal di ladang; namanya berarti \"makhluk gandum hitam\". Ia disebut roh kekayaan dan kesejahteraan yang membantu tanaman matang dan menjaga panen. Ia dibayangkan sebagai kakek kecil berjanggut panjang, \"Kakek Gandum Hitam\", dengan mata ketiga di tengkuk.",
      "en": "Zhytsen is a benevolent being of Belarusian mythology that lives in the fields; its name means \"rye creature\". It is called a spirit of wealth and wellbeing that helps crops ripen and cares for the harvest. It was imagined as a little long-bearded old man, the \"Rye Grandfather\", with a third eye on the back of its head.",

    {
      "id": "Zhytsen berjalan melintasi ladang memastikan panen dituai dengan baik, dan bulir yang ditinggalkan petani ceroboh ia ikat menjadi berkas lalu bawa ke ladang petani yang lebih rajin. Sosoknya masih muncul dalam ungkapan \"Zhytsen memberi roti\". Namun sebagai dewa kesuburan Belarus kuno, ia digambarkan dan kemungkinan dikarang oleh folkloris Pavel Shpilevsky.",
      "en": "Zhytsen walks the fields making sure crops are well reaped, and the grain left by careless farmers it binds into sheaves and carries to the fields of more diligent ones.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c01",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s1",
      "quote": "is a positive creature from Belarusian mythology said to live in fields. The name literally means \"rye creature\", from Belarusian жыта, \"rye\".",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zhytsen atau Žycień adalah makhluk baik dalam mitologi Belarus yang konon tinggal di ladang; namanya secara harfiah berarti \"makhluk gandum hitam\", dari kata Belarus zhyta, \"gandum hitam\".",
        "en": "Zhytsen or Žycień is a benevolent creature of Belarusian mythology said to live in fields; the name literally means \"rye creature\", from Belarusian zhyta, \"rye\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c02",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s1",
      "quote": "Zhyten is often called a spirit of wealth and wellbeing. It is believed that Zhytsen contributes to good ripening of crops and vegetables, and accordingly, Zhyten is considered the caretaker of good harvests.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zhytsen sering disebut roh kekayaan dan kesejahteraan; ia dipercaya membantu tanaman dan sayuran matang dengan baik, sehingga dianggap pengurus panen yang baik.",
        "en": "Zhytsen is often called a spirit of wealth and wellbeing; it is believed to help crops and vegetables ripen well and so is considered the caretaker of good harvests."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c03",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s1",
      "quote": "Zhytsen walks through the fields and ensures that crops there are well reaped. It is believed in Belarusian folklore that if Zhytsen finds some left ears of grain, it binds them in sheaves and brings to the fields of more diligent farmers.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zhytsen berjalan melintasi ladang memastikan panen dituai dengan baik; dalam folklor Belarus dipercaya bila ia menemukan bulir yang tertinggal, ia mengikatnya menjadi berkas dan membawanya ke ladang petani yang lebih rajin.",
        "en": "Zhytsen walks the fields making sure the crops are well reaped; Belarusian folklore holds that if it finds ears of grain left behind, it binds them into sheaves and carries them to the fields of more diligent farmers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c04",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s2",
      "quote": "Как древнебелорусский бог плодородия и осени описан (и, вероятно, придуман) фольклористом Павлом Шпилевским (Древлянским).",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai dewa kesuburan dan musim gugur Belarus kuno, Zhytsen digambarkan, dan kemungkinan dikarang, oleh folkloris Pavel Shpilevsky (Drevlyansky).",
        "en": "As an ancient Belarusian god of fertility and autumn, Zhytsen was described, and probably invented, by the folklorist Pavel Shpilevsky (Drevlyansky)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c05",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s2",
      "quote": "Особенностью Житеня является третий глаз на затылке, который нужен для лучшего выполнения задач этого мифологического персонажа.",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ciri khas Zhytsen adalah mata ketiga di tengkuk, yang membantunya menjalankan tugasnya dengan lebih baik.",
        "en": "A peculiarity of Zhytsen is a third eye on the back of the head, which helps it do its work better."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c06",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s3",
      "quote": "На Беларусі Жыценя уяўлялі маленькім дзедам («Жытні дзед») з доўгай барадой.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Belarus, Zhytsen dibayangkan sebagai kakek kecil (\"Kakek Gandum Hitam\") berjanggut panjang.",
        "en": "In Belarus Zhytsen was imagined as a little old man (\"Rye Grandfather\") with a long beard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "zhytsen-c07",
      "source_id": "zhytsen-s4",
      "quote": "Образ Жиценя й досі спостерігається в білоруському фольклорі, зокрема в примовці «Жыцень хлеба дав».",
      "locator": "вступ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok Zhytsen masih tampak dalam folklor Belarus, antara lain dalam ungkapan \"Zhytsen memberi roti\".",
        "en": "The figure of Zhytsen still appears in Belarusian folklore, for example in the saying \"Zhytsen gave bread\"."
      }
    }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Błędnica adalah demon perempuan hutan yang menyesatkan orang hingga mati di hutan, biasanya berwujud gadis muda yang cantik.",
    "en": "Błędnica is a forest demoness who leads people astray to die in the forest, usually in the form of a pretty young girl.",

    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Błędnica adalah demon perempuan hutan yang konon menyesatkan orang dan meninggalkan mereka sendirian di kedalaman hutan hingga mati kelaparan atau dimangsa binatang buas.   "traits": [],
```
```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dziwożona adalah demon rawa perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia, nenek berbulu berpayudara besar yang menculik bayi dan menggantinya dengan anak tukaran.",
    "en": "Dziwożona is a female swamp demon of Slavic mythology, a hairy, huge-breasted hag who steals babies and leaves changelings in their place.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dziwozona-c01",
      "dziwozona-c03",
      "dziwozona-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dziwożona, juga disebut Mamuna atau Boginka, adalah demon rawa perempuan yang jahat dalam mitologi Slavia. Yang paling berisiko menjadi demon ini setelah mati adalah bidan, perawan tua, ibu yang tidak menikah, perempuan hamil yang meninggal sebelum melahirkan, dan anak haram yang ditelantarkan. Namanya berarti \"perempuan liar\": kata Polandia ini pinjaman dari bahasa Slowakia, terutama dipakai penduduk pegunungan Tatra, dan dipopulerkan novel Zygmunt Kaczkowski tahun 1855.",
      "en": "Dziwożona, also called Mamuna or Boginka, is a malicious female swamp demon of Slavic mythology. Those most at risk of becoming one after death were midwives, old maids, unmarried mothers, pregnant women who died before childbirth and abandoned children born out of wedlock. Her name means \"wild woman\": the Polish word is a loan from Slovak, used especially by the Tatra highlanders and popularised by Zygmunt Kaczkowski's 1855 novel.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dziwozona-c01",
        "dziwozona-c02",
        "dziwozona-c07",
        "dziwozona-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia konon tinggal di semak dekat sungai dan danau, berwujud nenek buruk rupa bertubuh berbulu dengan payudara begitu besar hingga dipakai mencuci pakaian, dan memakai topi merah berhias ranting pakis. Ia menculik bayi yang baru lahir dan menggantinya dengan anaknya sendiri, si anak tukaran.",
      "en": "She was said to live in thickets by rivers and lakes as an ugly, hairy old woman with breasts so huge she washed clothes with them, wearing a red hat with a fern twig. She stole newborn babies and replaced them with her own child, the changeling.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dziwozona-c03",
        "dziwozona-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Untuk melindungi anak, ibu mengikat pita merah di tangannya, memakaikan topi merah, dan melindungi wajahnya dari cahaya bulan; kebiasaan pita merah masih bertahan di beberapa daerah Polandia. Bila bayi sudah tertukar, ibu membawa anak tukaran ke tumpukan sampah, memukulnya dengan ranting birch, dan menyiramnya dengan air dari cangkang telur sambil berteriak \"Ambil milikmu, kembalikan milikku!\", hingga Dziwożona mengembalikan bayi yang dicuri.",
      "en": "To protect a child, a mother tied a red ribbon round its hand, put a red hat on it and shielded its face from moonlight; the ribbon custom survives in some regions of Poland. If the baby had been swapped, the mother took the changeling to a midden, whipped it with a birch twig and poured water on it from an eggshell, shouting \"Take yours, give mine back!\", until Dziwożona returned the stolen child.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dziwozona-c05",
        "dziwozona-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "dziwozona-c01",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s1",
      "quote": "Dziwożona (or Mamuna or Boginka) is a female swamp demon in Slavic mythology known for being malicious and dangerous. Most at risk of becoming one of these demons after death were thought to be midwives, old maids, unmarried mothers, pregnant women who die before childbirth, as well as abandoned children born out of wedlock.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dziwożona (juga Mamuna atau Boginka) adalah demon rawa perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang jahat dan berbahaya; yang paling berisiko menjadi demon ini setelah mati adalah bidan, perawan tua, ibu yang tidak menikah, perempuan hamil yang meninggal sebelum melahirkan, dan anak haram yang ditelantarkan.",
        "en": "Dziwożona (or Mamuna or Boginka) is a malicious, dangerous female swamp demon of Slavic mythology; those thought most likely to become one after death were midwives, old maids, unmarried mothers, pregnant women who died before childbirth, and abandoned children born out of wedlock."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c02",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s1",
      "quote": "In Slovak she was called „diva lena”/ „divá žena”, in Czech „divoženka”, which means \"wild woman\".",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Slowakia ia disebut \"diva lena\" atau \"divá žena\", dan dalam bahasa Ceko \"divoženka\", yang berarti \"perempuan liar\".",
        "en": "In Slovak she was called \"diva lena\" or \"divá žena\", in Czech \"divoženka\", meaning \"wild woman\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c03",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s1",
      "quote": "Dziwożona was said to live in thickets near rivers, streams and lakes. According to some, she took the form of an ugly, old woman with a hairy body, long straight hair and breasts so huge that she used them to wash her clothes. On her head she wore a red hat with a fern twig attached to it.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dziwożona konon tinggal di semak dekat sungai, anak sungai, dan danau; menurut sebagian orang ia berwujud perempuan tua buruk rupa bertubuh berbulu, berambut panjang lurus, dan berpayudara begitu besar hingga dipakai mencuci pakaian, serta memakai topi merah berhias ranting pakis.",
        "en": "Dziwożona was said to live in thickets near rivers, streams and lakes; some said she was an ugly old woman with a hairy body, long straight hair and breasts so huge she used them to wash her clothes, wearing a red hat with a fern twig."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c04",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s1",
      "quote": "Dziwożona was said to kidnap human babies just after they were born and replace them with her own children, known as foundlings or changelings.",
      "locator": "Changelings",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dziwożona konon menculik bayi manusia segera setelah lahir dan menggantinya dengan anaknya sendiri, yang disebut anak tukaran (changeling).",
        "en": "Dziwożona was said to kidnap human babies right after birth and replace them with her own children, known as changelings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c05",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s1",
      "quote": "To protect a child against being kidnapped by Dziwożona, a mother had to tie a red ribbon around its hand (this custom is still preserved in some regions of Poland, although without the original meaning), put a red hat on its head and shield its face from the light of the moon.",
      "locator": "Protection",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk melindungi anak dari Dziwożona, ibu harus mengikat pita merah di tangannya (kebiasaan yang masih bertahan di beberapa daerah Polandia tanpa makna aslinya), memakaikan topi merah, dan melindungi wajahnya dari cahaya bulan.",
        "en": "To protect a child from Dziwożona, a mother had to tie a red ribbon round its hand (a custom still kept in some regions of Poland without its original meaning), put a red hat on its head and shield its face from moonlight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c06",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s1",
      "quote": "The mother had to take the changeling to a midden, whip it with a birch twig and pour water over it from an eggshell, shouting \"Take yours, give mine back!\", at which point Dziwożona normally felt sorry for her offspring and took it away, returning the one she stole.",
      "locator": "Changelings",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk mendapatkan bayinya kembali, ibu harus membawa anak tukaran ke tumpukan sampah, memukulnya dengan ranting birch, dan menyiramnya dengan air dari cangkang telur sambil berteriak \"Ambil milikmu, kembalikan milikku!\", lalu Dziwożona biasanya merasa iba pada anaknya, mengambilnya, dan mengembalikan bayi yang dicuri.",
        "en": "To get her baby back, the mother had to take the changeling to a midden, whip it with a birch twig and pour water on it from an eggshell, shouting \"Take yours, give mine back!\", whereupon Dziwożona usually pitied her offspring, took it away and returned the stolen child."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c07",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s2",
      "quote": "Wyraz „dziwożona” (dzika żona/kobieta) jest w języku polskim pożyczką z języka słowackiego: „diva lena”/ „divá žena” oznaczała „dziką kobietę”, a polski odpowiednik spopularyzowany został przez wydaną w 1855 powieść „Dziwożona” autorstwa Zygmunta Kaczkowskiego.",
      "locator": "Etymologia",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata \"dziwożona\" (\"perempuan liar\") adalah pinjaman bahasa Polandia dari bahasa Slowakia, dan dipopulerkan oleh novel \"Dziwożona\" karya Zygmunt Kaczkowski yang terbit tahun 1855.",
        "en": "The word \"dziwożona\" (\"wild woman\") is a Polish loan from Slovak, popularised by Zygmunt Kaczkowski's novel \"Dziwożona\", published in 1855."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dziwozona-c08",
      "source_id": "dziwozona-s2",
      "quote": "Wyraz „dziwożona” szczególnie stosowany był u górali tatrzańskich",
      "locator": "Etymologia",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata \"dziwożona\" terutama dipakai di kalangan penduduk pegunungan Tatra.",
        "en": "The word \"dziwożona\" was used especially among the Tatra highlanders."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en dan pl; kumpulan Siemieński dan Goszczyński yang dirujuk belum dibuka."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fext adalah mayat hidup dalam kepercayaan rakyat Ceko dan Slavia, orang kebal peluru yang jasadnya tidak membusuk, dan hanya bisa dilukai peluru kaca.",
    "en": "The fext is an undead of Slavic folk belief known among Czechs, a bullet-proof person whose body does not decay, vulnerable only to glass bullets.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fext-c01",
      "fext-c02",
      "fext-c03"
    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fext adalah makhluk mayat hidup dalam mitologi Slavia yang asal-usulnya terkait Perang Tiga Puluh Tahun pada abad ke-17. Dalam bahasa Ceko, fext (juga fekst atau fest, mungkin dari kata Jerman fest, \"kokoh\") adalah sebutan rakyat bagi orang yang jasadnya tidak membusuk setelah mati, hanya mengering dan tetap utuh. Legenda tentangnya terutama berasal dari daerah Rychnov.",
      "en": "The fext is an undead creature of Slavic mythology whose origins are tied to the Thirty Years' War of the 17th century. As a folk term among Czechs, fext (also fekst or fest, probably from German fest, \"firm\") means a person whose body does not decay after death but merely dries and stays intact. Legends about it come mainly from the Rychnov region.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fext-c01",
        "fext-c03",
        "fext-c04",
        "fext-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Fext dipercaya kebal peluru, kecuali peluru kaca, dan beberapa jenderal besar masa itu dijuluki fext karena dianggap tak bisa mati. Menurut sebagian kesaksian, fext lahir dengan selaput ganda yang salah satunya dijahitkan di bawah ketiak bayi; menurut yang lain, kekebalannya didapat lewat sihir atau perjanjian dengan setan. Legenda ini di Eropa hanya dikenal orang Ceko, Slowakia, Jerman, dan Swedia.",
      "en": "The fext was believed invulnerable to bullets except glass ones, and some great generals of the time were called fexts for their supposed immortality. By some accounts a fext was born in a double caul, one of which was sewn under the infant's arm; by others, the invulnerability came from a spell or a pact with the devil. In Europe the legend is known only among Czechs, Slovaks, Germans and Swedes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fext-c02",

        "fext-c06",
        "fext-c07"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "fext-c01",
      "source_id": "fext-s1",
      "quote": "A Fext is a mythical undead creature in Slavic mythology. Its origins are found in the terrors of the Thirty Years' War (17th century) in central Europe.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fext adalah makhluk mayat hidup mitos dalam mitologi Slavia yang asal-usulnya berkaitan dengan kengerian Perang Tiga Puluh Tahun (abad ke-17) di Eropa Tengah.",
        "en": "A fext is a mythical undead creature of Slavic mythology whose origins lie in the terrors of the Thirty Years' War (17th century) in central Europe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fext-c02",
      "source_id": "fext-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that the Fext is invincible to bullets, except bullets made of glass. Some of the great generals of that time were called Fexts because of their assumed immortality.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon fext kebal terhadap peluru, kecuali peluru dari kaca, dan beberapa jenderal besar masa itu dijuluki fext karena dianggap tak bisa mati.",
        "en": "The fext is said to be invulnerable to bullets except those made of glass, and some great generals of the time were called fexts because of their supposed immortality."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fext-c03",
      "source_id": "fext-s2",
      "quote": "Fext (též fekst či fest, asi z německého fest, pevný, podle nezranitelnosti) je lidové označení pro člověka, jehož ostatky se po smrti nerozložily, jen seschly a setrvávaly v neporušené podobě.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fext (juga fekst atau fest, mungkin dari kata Jerman fest, \"kokoh\", karena kekebalannya) adalah sebutan rakyat bagi orang yang jasadnya tidak membusuk setelah mati, hanya mengering dan tetap utuh.",
        "en": "Fext (also fekst or fest, probably from German fest, \"firm\", for its invulnerability) is a folk term for a person whose remains did not decay after death but merely dried and stayed intact."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fext-c04",
      "source_id": "fext-s2",
      "quote": "Lidová slovesnost často fextům připisovala nadpřirozené schopnosti, kterými měli disponovat již za svého života, zpravidla to byla nezranitelnost. Pověsti o fextech pocházely obvykle z Rychnovska a okolí.",
      "locator": "úvod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi lisan sering memberi fext kemampuan gaib yang sudah dimiliki semasa hidup, biasanya kekebalan; legenda tentang fext umumnya berasal dari daerah Rychnov dan sekitarnya.",
        "en": "Oral tradition often credited fexts with supernatural powers they already had in life, usually invulnerability; legends of fexts mostly came from the Rychnov region and its surroundings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fext-c05",
      "source_id": "fext-s2",
      "quote": "Podle výpovědí paní Luňáčkové a Fr. Malátka z Velké Skrovnice přišel fext na svět ve dvojím obalu. Jeden byl po narození spálen, druhý sbalen do klubíčka a zašit novorozeněti pod paži.",
      "locator": "Vznik",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kesaksian Ibu Luňáčková dan Fr. Malátek dari Velká Skrovnice, fext lahir dengan selaput ganda: satu dibakar setelah kelahiran, yang lain digulung dan dijahitkan di bawah ketiak bayi.",
        "en": "According to the accounts of Mrs Luňáčková and Fr. Malátek of Velká Skrovnice, a fext was born in a double caul: one was burned after birth, the other rolled up and sewn under the newborn's arm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fext-c06",
      "source_id": "fext-s2",
      "quote": "Také se ale říkalo, že nezranitelnosti bylo dosaženo nějakým kouzlem nebo upsáním duše ďáblovi.",
      "locator": "Vznik",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namun dikatakan pula bahwa kekebalan itu diperoleh dengan sihir atau dengan menjual jiwa kepada setan.",
        "en": "It was also said, however, that invulnerability was gained through some spell or by signing one's soul over to the devil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fext-c07",
      "source_id": "fext-s2",
      "quote": "Zajímavé je, že pověsti o fextech znají v Evropě jen Češi (+ Slováci), Němci a Švédové. Může to souviset se vznikem tohoto mýtu v dobách třicetileté vál",
      "locator": "Rozšíření",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda tentang fext di Eropa hanya dikenal oleh orang Ceko (dan Slowakia), Jerman, dan Swedia, yang mungkin berkaitan dengan munculnya mitos ini pada masa Perang Tiga Puluh Tahun.",
        "en": "Legends of fexts are known in Europe only among Czechs (and Slovaks), Germans and Swedes, which may relate to the myth's origin in the Thirty Years' War."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rubah Api (tulikettu) adalah rubah mitos dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia utara dan timur yang ekornya memercikkan api dan konon menimbulkan cahaya utara.",
    "en": "The firefox (tulikettu) is a mythical fox of northern and eastern Finnish folklore whose fiery tail is said to cause the northern lights.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "firefox-mythology-c01",
      "firefox-mythology-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rubah Api, dalam bahasa Finlandia tulikettu, tulirepo, atau tulikko, adalah makhluk mitos dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia utara dan timur: seekor rubah yang ekornya memercikkan api. Ia tinggal jauh di persembunyian di hutan atau di utara, dan sangat sedikit yang pernah melihatnya. Siang hari ia hitam, malam hari ia berkilau api.",
      "en": "The firefox, in Finnish tulikettu, tulirepo or tulikko, is a mythical creature of northern and eastern Finnish folklore: a fox whose tail sparkles with fire. It lives far off in woodland hideouts or in the north, and very few have seen it. By day it is black; by night it glitters with fire.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "firefox-mythology-c01",
        "firefox-mythology-c02",
        "firefox-mythology-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kulitnya dianggap sangat berharga dan konon dipakai menerangi gudang mesiu tanpa api terbuka, sehingga pemburu memimpikan menangkapnya agar kaya seumur hidup. Cahaya utara dalam bahasa Finlandia disebut \"api rubah\" (revontulet); di Lapland diceritakan bahwa bunga api dari ekornya saat menyentuh dahan rendah menimbulkan cahaya utara. Pada 2025 NASA menerbitkan foto rubah di bawah aurora dari Kilpisjärvi dengan rujukan ke mitos ini.",
      "en": "Its hide was considered immensely valuable and was said to light gunpowder stores without open flame, so hunters dreamed of catching one to be rich for life. In Finnish the northern lights are \"fox's fires\" (revontulet); in Lapland it is told that sparks from its tail brushing low branches create the aurora.       "id": "Gulyabani adalah makhluk pengubah wujud, roh jahat rendahan dalam kepercayaan orang Azerbaijan, Turki, Kirgiz, dan Tajik. Namanya berasal dari kata Arab ghul (\"monster-demon\") dan kata Persia yaban (\"padang gurun\"). Dalam mitologi Azerbaijan ia digambarkan sebagai makhluk liar yang buas dan berbulu; padanannya, Quyux, muncul dalam gelap di padang dan pemakaman, berbulu kuning kemerahan, berbau busuk, dan sangat besar.",
```
```json
      "en": "The gulyabani is a shapeshifter, a lesser evil spirit in the beliefs of the Azerbaijanis, Turks, Kyrgyz and Tajiks. Its name comes from Arabic ghul (\"monster-demon\") and Persian yaban (\"desert\"). In Azerbaijani mythology it is a wild, predatory, hairy being; its counterpart Quyux appears in the dark in steppes and cemeteries, covered in yellowish-red hair, foul-smelling and very large.",

      "quote": "(азерб. Qulyabani — «оборотень», с араб. гул — «чудовище-демон», с перс. ябан — «пустыня») — оборотень, низший злой дух в представлении азербайджанцев (гул-ябаны, гули-ябаны, биабан-гули), турок, киргизов (гульбиябан), таджиков (гул, гул-ёвони).",

        "en": "Gulyabani (Azerbaijani Qulyabani, \"werewolf\"; from Arabic ghul, \"monster-demon\", and Persian yaban, \"desert\") is a shapeshifter, a lesser evil spirit in the beliefs of the Azerbaijanis, Turks, Kyrgyz and Tajiks."

    {
      "id": "gulyabani-c02",
      "source_id": "gulyabani-s1",
      "quote": "По представлениям азербайджанцев и турок, гульябани был злым духом, жившим в степи или на кладбище. Его также можно было встретить в подземельях, руинах, на местах битв и побоищ. Излюбленным его занятием было пугать припозднившихся ночных путников, странников из караванов.",
      "locator": "Гульябани у азербайджанцев и турок",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "According to Azerbaijanis and Turks, the gulyabani was an evil spirit living in the steppe or a cemetery, also met in underground places, ruins and old battlefields; its favourite pastime was frightening belated night travellers and caravan wanderers."

    },
    {
      "id": "gulyabani-c03",
      "source_id": "gulyabani-s1",

По поверью если его поймать и воткнуть в ворот его одежды иголку, он, подобно рабу («гул» в переводе с азербайджанского означает «раб»), станет работать на человека.       "statement": {

        "en": "In Azerbaijani belief the gulyabani had werewolf traits, liking to ride horses at night and tangle their manes; if caught and a needle stuck in the collar of its clothes, it would work like a slave (\"gul\" in Azerbaijani means \"slave\"), but would carry out all its master's orders backwards."

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel Azerbaijan, padanannya Quyux berarti makhluk liar; menurut gambaran paling terkenal ia muncul dalam gelap di padang dan pemakaman, tubuhnya tertutup bulu kuning kemerahan, berbau busuk, dan sangat besar.",
        "en": "According to the Azerbaijani article, its counterpart Quyux means a wild being; in the best-known description it appears in the dark in the steppes and cemeteries, its body covered in yellowish-red hair, foul-smelling and very large."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gulyabani-c05",
      "source_id": "gulyabani-s2",
      "quote": "Qulyabanı (fars. غول یابانی‎ və ya غول بیابانی ğoul-e yabani və ya ğoul-e biyabani – \"vəhşi nəhəng və ya düzənlik nəhəngi”) — Azərbaycan mifologiyasında vəhşi heyvan, fövqəladə, yırtıcı canlı, tüklü varlıq.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Azerbaijan, Qulyabanı (dari bahasa Persia ghoul-e yabani atau ghoul-e biyabani, \"raksasa liar\" atau \"raksasa padang\") adalah binatang liar, makhluk luar biasa yang buas dan berbulu.",
        "en": "In Azerbaijani mythology Qulyabanı (from Persian ghoul-e yabani or ghoul-e biyabani, \"wild giant\" or \"giant of the plains\") is a wild beast, an extraordinary, predatory, hairy being."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
    {
      "id": "Hiiden hirvi, \"rusa besar milik Hiisi\", adalah rusa besar mitos dalam mitologi Finlandia yang harus diburu dengan bermain ski; mitosnya menceritakan penciptaan ski dan asal-usul rusa besar. Dalam epos Kalevala ia hewan gaib yang sulit ditangkap, cepat, dan kuat, dan Louhi menugaskan para pelamar putrinya menangkapnya hidup-hidup. Padanannya di Estonia adalah Hiiehobune, \"kuda milik Hiis\".",
      "en": "Hiiden hirvi, \"Hiisi's moose\", is a mythical moose of Finnish mythology that must be hunted on skis; its myth tells the creation of skis and the origin of the moose.   "short_description": {
```
```json
    "id": "Kłobuk adalah roh rumah dalam kepercayaan Polandia, terutama di Warmia, yang berwujud ayam basah dan memperkaya tuannya dengan mencuri dari tetangga.",
    "en": "The kłobuk is a house spirit of Polish belief, especially in Warmia, that takes the form of a drenched hen and enriches its master by robbing the neighbours.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "k-obuk-c01",
      "k-obuk-c02",
      "k-obuk-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kłobuk atau kołbuk adalah demon penjaga harta dan perapian rumah dalam kepercayaan Slavia, disamakan dengan jiwa janin yang mati. Ia paling sering berwujud ayam betina basah, tetapi juga bebek, angsa, murai, gagak, kucing, atau manusia. Kepercayaan ini hidup terutama di Warmia selatan dan wilayah berbahasa Polandia di bekas Prusia Timur, juga di Masuria dan Silesia.",
      "en": "The kłobuk or kołbuk is a demon guarding property and the household hearth in Slavic belief, identified with the soul of a dead foetus. It most often takes the form of a drenched hen, but also a duck, goose, magpie, crow, cat or human. The belief lived mainly in southern Warmia and the Polish-speaking parts of former East Prussia, as well as in Masuria and Silesia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "k-obuk-c01",
        "k-obuk-c04",
        "k-obuk-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kłobuk dipercaya bisa ditetaskan dari telur ayam hitam atau \"telur ayam jantan\", ditemukan sebagai anak ayam basah setelah hujan lebat, atau dibeli dari orang yang tahu. Ia memperkaya tuannya dengan mencuri dari tetangga, menuntut perawatan serta kamar khusus bercat hitam di loteng, dan bisa membalas dendam bila diperlakukan buruk.",
      "en": "A kłobuk was believed to be hatched from a black hen's egg or a \"cock's egg\", found as a wet chick after heavy rain, or bought from those in the know. It enriched its master by robbing the neighbours, demanded care and a black-painted room in the attic, and could take revenge if badly treated.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "k-obuk-c02",
        "k-obuk-c03",
        "k-obuk-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "k-obuk-c01",
      "source_id": "k-obuk-s1",
      "quote": "Kłobuk ist der Name eines Kobolds, dessen Geschichten im südlichen Ermland (Powiat Olsztyński in der polnischen Woiwodschaft Ermland-Masuren) erzählt werden. Es handelt sich um einen Geist, der in Gestalt eines gefiederten Wesens, einer Katze oder als Mensch auftritt.",
      "locator": "Einleitung",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kłobuk adalah nama kobold (roh rumah) yang kisahnya diceritakan di Warmia selatan (Kabupaten Olsztyn, Provinsi Warmia-Masuria, Polandia); ia roh yang muncul sebagai makhluk berbulu unggas, kucing, atau manusia.",
        "en": "Kłobuk is the name of a kobold (house spirit) whose stories are told in southern Warmia (Olsztyn County in Poland's Warmian-Masurian Voivodeship); it is a spirit appearing as a feathered creature, a cat or a human."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-obuk-c02",
      "source_id": "k-obuk-s1",
      "quote": "Obwohl er als lästig beschrieben wird, bringt er dem Haushalt Glück und Wohlstand, oftmals indem er die Nachbarn des Besitzers bestiehlt.",
      "locator": "Einleitung",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meski digambarkan merepotkan, ia membawa keberuntungan dan kemakmuran bagi rumah tangga, sering dengan mencuri dari tetangga pemiliknya.",
        "en": "Though described as a nuisance, it brings the household luck and prosperity, often by stealing from the owner's neighbours."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-obuk-c03",
      "source_id": "k-obuk-s2",
      "quote": "Считалось, что духа-обогатителя можно вывести из особого яйца (чёрной курицы или из «петушиного»), либо просто найти случайно после сильного ливня в виде мокрого цыплёнка, птенца (либо даже купить у «знающих» людей)",
      "locator": "вступление",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh pembawa kekayaan ini dipercaya bisa ditetaskan dari telur khusus (telur ayam hitam atau \"telur ayam jantan\"), ditemukan secara kebetulan setelah hujan lebat dalam wujud anak ayam basah, atau bahkan dibeli dari orang yang \"tahu\".",
        "en": "This enriching spirit was believed to be hatched from a special egg (of a black hen or a \"cock's egg\"), found by chance after heavy rain as a wet chick, or even bought from people \"in the know\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-obuk-c04",
      "source_id": "k-obuk-s3",
      "quote": "Kłobuk, kołbuk – w wierzeniach słowiańskich demon opiekujący się dobytkiem i ogniskiem domowym. Utożsamiany z duszą martwego płodu. Przybierał najczęściej postać zmokłej kury, a także kaczki, gęsi, sroki, wrony, kota, a nawet człowieka.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kłobuk atau kołbuk dalam kepercayaan Slavia adalah demon penjaga harta dan perapian rumah, disamakan dengan jiwa janin yang mati, dan paling sering berwujud ayam betina basah, juga bebek, angsa, murai, gagak, kucing, bahkan manusia.",
        "en": "Kłobuk or kołbuk is, in Slavic belief, a demon guarding property and the household hearth, identified with the soul of a dead foetus, most often taking the form of a drenched hen, but also a duck, goose, magpie, crow, cat or even a human."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-obuk-c05",
      "source_id": "k-obuk-s3",
      "quote": "Wiara w kłobuki żywa była głównie w południowych (polskojęzycznych) rejonach dawnych Prus Wschodnich, przede wszystkim wśród Warmiaków (z południowej Warmii), w mniejszym zakresie wśród Mazurów, jak również na Śląsku.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan pada kłobuk hidup terutama di wilayah selatan (berbahasa Polandia) bekas Prusia Timur, terutama di kalangan orang Warmia selatan, lebih sedikit di kalangan orang Masuria, serta di Silesia.",
        "en": "Belief in kłobuks was alive mainly in the southern (Polish-speaking) areas of former East Prussia, above all among the Warmians of southern Warmia, less among the Masurians, and also in Silesia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-obuk-c06",
      "source_id": "k-obuk-s3",
      "quote": "Kłobuk dbał o pomnożenie majątku swojego gospodarza. Czynił to jednak, okradając sąsiadów. Od swojego właściciela wymagał opieki, a także osobnego, pomalowanego na czarno pokoju, znajdującego się na końcu strychu lub w innym, trudno dostępnym miejscu. Źle potraktowany, może się mścić.",
      "locator": "wstęp",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kłobuk melipatgandakan harta tuannya, tetapi dengan mencuri dari tetangga; ia menuntut perawatan dan kamar khusus bercat hitam di ujung loteng atau tempat sulit dijangkau, dan bila diperlakukan buruk ia bisa membalas dendam.",
        "en": "The kłobuk increased its master's wealth, but by robbing the neighbours; it demanded care and a separate black-painted room at the end of the attic or another hard-to-reach place, and if badly treated it could take revenge."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia de, ru, dan pl."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
    {
      "id": "Kanjirottu Yakshi adalah dewi rakyat India Selatan. Menurut mitos ia lahir sebagai Chiruthevi dalam keluarga Mangalathu di Kanjiracode, Travancore Selatan, seorang pelacur istana jelita. Ia jatuh cinta kepada pemikul tandunya, Kunjuraman, yang sudah menikah, lalu mengatur pembunuhan istri Kunjuraman; sebagai balasan Kunjuraman mencekiknya di ranjang.",
      "en": "Kanjirottu Yakshi is a folk deity of South India. By the myth she was born as Chiruthevi into the Mangalathu family at Kanjiracode in Southern Travancore, a beautiful courtesan. She fell in love with her married palanquin-bearer Kunjuraman and had his wife killed; in revenge Kunjuraman strangled her in bed.",

      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kanjirottu Yakshi adalah dewi rakyat India Selatan; menurut mitos ia lahir dalam keluarga Mangalathu di Kanjiracode, Travancore Selatan (kini Tamil Nadu), dikenal juga sebagai Chiruthevi, seorang pelacur istana jelita; ia menjadi yakshi setelah dibunuh pemikul tandunya, lalu memikat lelaki dengan kecantikannya dan meminum darah mereka.",
        "en": "Kanjirottu Yakshi is a folk deity of South India; by the myth she was born into the Mangalathu family at Kanjiracode in Southern Travancore (now Tamil Nadu), also called Chiruthevi, a beautiful courtesan; she became a yakshi after being murdered by her palanquin-bearer, waylaying men with her beauty and drinking their blood."
      }

      "id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-c02",
      "source_id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-s1",
      "quote": "However, she fell in love with Kunjuraman, her palanquin-bearer, who would carry both her and her brother Govindan around on his back. However, Kunjuraman was already married and did not reciprocate Chiruthevi's feelings.",
      "locator": "Legend",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia jatuh cinta kepada Kunjuraman, pemikul tandunya yang biasa memikul dia dan saudaranya Govindan, tetapi Kunjuraman sudah menikah dan tidak membalas perasaannya.",
        "en": "She fell in love with Kunjuraman, her palanquin-bearer, who carried her and her brother Govindan, but Kunjuraman was already married and did not return her feelings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-c03",
      "source_id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-s1",
      "quote": "Frustrated by Kunjuraman's romantic indifference towards her, Chiruthevi arranged for Kunjuraman's wife to be killed. While they were in bed, Kunjuraman strangled Chiruthevi in order to avenge the murder of his wife.",
      "locator": "Legend",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kecewa, Chiruthevi mengatur pembunuhan istri Kunjuraman; ketika Govindan mengetahuinya dan memberi tahu Kunjuraman, Kunjuraman bersedia tidur dengan Chiruthevi lalu mencekiknya di ranjang untuk membalas kematian istrinya.",
        "en": "Frustrated, Chiruthevi had Kunjuraman's wife killed; when Govindan found out and told Kunjuraman, Kunjuraman agreed to sleep with Chiruthevi and strangled her in bed to avenge his wife."
      }

    {
      "id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-c04",
      "source_id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-s1",
      "quote": "Following her murder, Chiruthevi was reborn as a yakshi to a couple in Kanjirottu. She magically transformed into a bewitchingly beautiful woman moments after her birth. As a yakshi, she proceeded to seduce and terrorize men and drink their blood, never forgetting to harass the still-living Kunjuraman, whom she still desired.",
      "locator": "Legend",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah dibunuh, Chiruthevi lahir kembali sebagai yakshi dari pasangan di Kanjirottu dan sesaat setelah lahir berubah secara gaib menjadi perempuan yang memikat; sebagai yakshi ia menggoda dan meneror lelaki serta meminum darah mereka, dan terus mengganggu Kunjuraman yang masih ia inginkan.",
        "en": "After her murder Chiruthevi was reborn as a yakshi to a couple in Kanjirottu and moments after birth magically became a bewitching woman; as a yakshi she seduced and terrorised men and drank their blood, never ceasing to harass Kunjuraman, whom she still desired."

      "source_id": "kanjirottu-yakshi-s1",
      "quote": "Folklore states that, she sought refuge in the Sree Padmanabhaswamy Temple. Today, she is worshipped in a small shrine (Thekkedom) within the temple complex, where she is seen as a protector deity who has traded her thirst for blood for spiritual devotion.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Folklore says she took refuge in the Sree Padmanabhaswamy Temple; today she is worshipped in a small shrine (Thekkedom) within the temple complex as a protector deity who traded her thirst for blood for spiritual devotion."
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lidérc adalah demon khas cerita rakyat Hungaria yang muncul sebagai anak ayam ajaib, setan kecil pembawa kekayaan, atau kekasih setan yang terbang sebagai cahaya api.",
    "en": "The lidérc is a demon unique to Hungarian folklore, appearing as a miracle chicken, a wealth-bringing little devil or a satanic lover flying as a fiery light.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "liderc-c01",
      "liderc-c04",
      "liderc-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lidérc adalah demon mitologi Hungaria yang tersebar luas dalam tradisi rakyat, dengan banyak nama seperti lüdérc, lúdvérc, dan inglice. Ia punya tiga ragam yang sering saling meminjam ciri. Bentuk yang lebih tradisional adalah anak ayam ajaib yang menetas dari telur pertama ayam hitam yang dihangatkan di bawah ketiak manusia; ia menimbun emas sehingga pemiliknya kaya.",
      "en": "The lidérc is a demon of Hungarian mythology widespread in folk tradition, with many names such as lüdérc, lúdvérc and inglice. It has three varieties that often borrow traits from one another. The more traditional form is a miracle chicken hatched from a black hen's first egg kept warm under a person's arm; it hoards gold and makes its owner rich.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "liderc-c01",
        "liderc-c03",
        "liderc-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lidérc melekat pada manusia sebagai kekasih, duduk di tubuh korbannya dan kadang mengisap darahnya hingga lemah dan sakit; dari sini muncul kata Hungaria untuk mimpi buruk, lidércnyomás. Ragam lain adalah kekasih setan yang mirip incubus atau succubus dan terbang pada malam hari sebagai cahaya berapi atau burung api.",
      "en": "The lidérc attaches itself to people as a lover, sitting on its victim's body and sometimes sucking blood until the victim grows weak and ill; hence the Hungarian word for nightmare, lidércnyomás. Another variety is a satanic lover much like an incubus or succubus that flies at night as a fiery light or a bird of fire.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "liderc-c02",
        "liderc-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Betapapun bergunanya, lidérc harus segera disingkirkan karena sifatnya jahat. Caranya dengan membujuknya mengerjakan tugas mustahil, seperti mengangkut pasir dengan tali atau air dengan saringan, atau mengurungnya dalam lubang pohon.",
      "en": "However useful it may be, the lidérc must be got rid of quickly because of its evil nature. This is done by persuading it to do an impossible task, such as hauling sand with a rope or water with a sieve, or by locking it in a tree hollow.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "liderc-c03",
        "liderc-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

  ],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "liderc-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lid%C3%A9rc",
      "title": "Lidérc",

    },
    {
      "id": "liderc-s2",
      "url": "https://hu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lid%C3%A9rc",
      "title": "Lidérc",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (hu)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "hu",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "liderc-c01",
      "source_id": "liderc-s1",
      "quote": "is a unique supernatural being of Hungarian folklore. It has three known varieties, which often borrow traits from one another. The first, more traditional form of the lidérc is as a miracle chicken, csodacsirke in Hungarian, which hatches from the first egg of a black hen kept warm under the arm of a human.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lidérc adalah makhluk gaib khas cerita rakyat Hungaria dengan tiga ragam yang sering saling meminjam ciri; bentuk pertama yang lebih tradisional adalah anak ayam ajaib (csodacsirke) yang menetas dari telur pertama ayam hitam yang dihangatkan di bawah ketiak manusia.",
        "en": "The lidérc is a unique supernatural being of Hungarian folklore with three varieties that often borrow traits from one another; the first, more traditional form is a miracle chicken (csodacsirke) hatched from a black hen's first egg kept warm under a person's arm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "liderc-c02",
      "source_id": "liderc-s1",
      "quote": "The lidérc attaches itself to people to become their lover. ... it fondles her, sits on her body, and sometimes sucks her blood, making her weak and sick after a time. From this source comes a Hungarian word for nightmare -- lidércnyomás, which literally means \"lidérc pressure\", from the pressure on the body while the being sits on it.",
      "locator": "Varieties",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lidérc melekat pada manusia untuk menjadi kekasihnya; ia menggerayangi korbannya, duduk di tubuhnya, kadang mengisap darahnya hingga perempuan itu lemah dan sakit; dari sinilah kata Hungaria untuk mimpi buruk, lidércnyomás, \"tekanan lidérc\".",
        "en": "The lidérc attaches itself to people as their lover; fondling its victim, sitting on her body and sometimes sucking her blood until she grows weak and ill; hence the Hungarian word for nightmare, lidércnyomás, \"lidérc pressure\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "liderc-c03",
      "source_id": "liderc-s1",
      "quote": "The lidérc hoards gold and thus makes its owner rich. To dispose of this form of the lidérc, it must be persuaded to perform an impossible task, such as haul sand with rope, or water with a sieve. It can also be destroyed by locking it into a tree hollow.",
      "locator": "Varieties",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lidérc menimbun emas sehingga pemiliknya kaya; untuk menyingkirkannya, ia harus dibujuk mengerjakan tugas mustahil, seperti mengangkut pasir dengan tali atau air dengan saringan, atau dimusnahkan dengan dikurung dalam lubang pohon.",
        "en": "The lidérc hoards gold and so makes its owner rich; to get rid of it, it must be persuaded to do an impossible task, such as hauling sand with a rope or water with a sieve, or it can be destroyed by locking it in a tree hollow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "liderc-c04",
      "source_id": "liderc-s1",
      "quote": "The third variety is as a Satanic lover, ördögszerető in Hungarian, quite similar to an incubus or succubus. This form of the lidérc flies at night, appearing as a fiery light, a will o' the wisp, or even as a bird of fire.",
      "locator": "Varieties",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ragam ketiga adalah kekasih setan (ördögszerető), mirip incubus atau succubus, yang terbang pada malam hari sebagai cahaya berapi, api tipuan, atau burung api.",
        "en": "The third variety is the satanic lover (ördögszerető), much like an incubus or succubus, which flies at night as a fiery light, a will-o'-the-wisp or a bird of fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "liderc-c05",
      "source_id": "liderc-s2",
      "quote": "A lidérc (lüdérc, lúdvérc, lederc, lidroc, lidröc, inglice stb) a magyar mitológia démona, mely a néphagyományban szélesen elterjedt.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lidérc (juga lüdérc, lúdvérc, lederc, lidroc, lidröc, inglice, dan lain-lain) adalah demon mitologi Hungaria yang tersebar luas dalam tradisi rakyat.",
        "en": "The lidérc (also lüdérc, lúdvérc, lederc, lidroc, lidröc, inglice and so on) is a demon of Hungarian mythology widespread in folk tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "liderc-c06",
      "source_id": "liderc-s2",
      "quote": "Az elnevezések különbözőek lehetnek, de minden nép folklórja megegyezik abban, bármilyen hasznos is lehet, gonosz természete miatt minél hamarabb meg kell szabadulni tőle.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya bisa berbeda-beda, tetapi folklor semua bangsa sepakat bahwa betapapun bergunanya, karena sifatnya yang jahat ia harus disingkirkan secepat mungkin.",
        "en": "The names may differ, but every people's folklore agrees that, however useful it may be, because of its evil nature it must be got rid of as soon as possible."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en dan hu; artikel sr tidak dikutip. Kutipan novel di artikel en tidak dipakai."
    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Maa-alused adalah makhluk kecil mirip peri dalam agama rakyat Estonia yang hidup di bawah tanah dalam dunia sejajar yang serba terbalik.",
    "en": "Maa-alused are small elf-like beings of Estonian folk religion living beneath the ground in a parallel, reversed world.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "maa-alused-c01",
      "maa-alused-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Maa-alused adalah makhluk misterius mirip peri dalam agama rakyat Estonia yang hidup di bawah tanah atau di bawah lantai; semula mereka dianggap makhluk yang sudah mati. Mereka hidup sejajar dengan manusia, tetapi semua arah terbalik, atas menjadi bawah dan kiri menjadi kanan, dan semua milik mereka berukuran lebih kecil.",
      "en": "Maa-alused are mysterious elf-like beings of Estonian folk religion living under the ground or under the floor; originally they were held to be dead beings. They live parallel to humans, but all orientations are reversed, up becoming down and left becoming right, and everything they own is smaller.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "maa-alused-c01",
        "maa-alused-c02",
        "maa-alused-c03",
        "maa-alused-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Terutama di Estonia Utara, mereka dikenal sebagai makhluk kecil berwujud manusia yang keberadaannya ditandai suara; mereka bekerja sebagai pandai besi dan penenun, muncul sebagai serangga atau katak, dan membalas kebaikan dengan murah hati. Membuat mereka marah bisa mendatangkan penyakit dari tanah, sehingga di Saaremaa orang tidak boleh menginjak serangga atau jalur mereka.",
      "en": "Mainly in North Estonia they were known as tiny human-shaped beings whose presence was signalled by sounds; they worked as smiths and weavers, appeared as insects or frogs, and repaid kindness generously. Angering them could bring a disease from the earth, so on Saaremaa people were not to step on insects or their paths.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "maa-alused-c05",
        "maa-alused-c06"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "maa-alused-c01",
      "source_id": "maa-alused-s1",
      "quote": "Maa-alused are, in Estonian folk religion, mysterious elf-like creatures which live beneath the ground.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam agama rakyat Estonia, maa-alused adalah makhluk misterius mirip peri yang hidup di bawah tanah.",
        "en": "In Estonian folk religion, maa-alused are mysterious elf-like creatures that live beneath the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maa-alused-c02",
      "source_id": "maa-alused-s1",
      "quote": "They were believed to have a parallel existence to that of humans, the principal differences being that all orientations are reversed, such that up becomes down and left becomes right, and that all things possessed by them are diminished in size.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dipercaya hidup sejajar dengan manusia, dengan perbedaan utama bahwa semua arah terbalik, atas menjadi bawah dan kiri menjadi kanan, dan semua milik mereka berukuran lebih kecil.",
        "en": "They were believed to live a parallel existence to humans, the main differences being that all orientations are reversed, up becoming down and left becoming right, and that everything they own is smaller in size."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maa-alused-c03",
      "source_id": "maa-alused-s2",
      "quote": "Maa-alused eram, na mitologia estoniana, misteriosas criaturas élficas, as quais viviam sob a terra.",
      "locator": "introdução",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Portugis juga menyebut maa-alused sebagai makhluk misterius mirip peri dalam mitologia Estonia yang hidup di bawah tanah.",
        "en": "The Portuguese article likewise calls maa-alused mysterious elfin creatures of Estonian mythology living under the earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maa-alused-c04",
      "source_id": "maa-alused-s3",
      "quote": "Maa-alused (ka maahaldjad) olid algselt surnud olendid, kes hiljem elasid maa või põranda all.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maa-alused (juga disebut maahaldjad) semula adalah makhluk yang sudah mati, yang kemudian hidup di bawah tanah atau di bawah lantai.",
        "en": "Maa-alused (also maahaldjad) were originally dead beings who later lived under the ground or under the floor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maa-alused-c05",
      "source_id": "maa-alused-s3",
      "quote": "Maa-alused olid tuntud pisikeste inimesekujuliste mütoloogiliste olenditena põhiliselt Põhja-Eestis, nende olemasolust andsid märku hääled. Nad tegutsesid seppade (taovad tiks-tiks) ja kangrutena, maa peale ilmusid ka putuka või loomana (harilikult konnana). Neile tehtud heategude eest tasusid rikkalikult.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maa-alused dikenal sebagai makhluk mitos kecil berwujud manusia, terutama di Estonia Utara (Põhja-Eesti), yang keberadaannya ditandai suara-suara; mereka bekerja sebagai pandai besi (berbunyi tiks-tiks) dan penenun, muncul di atas tanah sebagai serangga atau hewan (biasanya katak), dan membalas kebaikan dengan murah hati.",
        "en": "Maa-alused were known as tiny human-shaped mythical beings mainly in North Estonia (Põhja-Eesti), whose presence was signalled by sounds; they worked as smiths (hammering tiks-tiks) and weavers, appeared above ground as insects or animals (usually frogs), and repaid good deeds generously."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maa-alused-c06",
      "source_id": "maa-alused-s3",
      "quote": "Maa-aluste ja maahaldjate (kes esinesid Lõuna-Eesti rahvapärimustes) pahandamine võis kaasa tuua maast saadud haiguse (haigust nimetati samuti maa-alused). Peamiselt Saaremaal ei olnud lubatud putukate peale või nende teeradadele astumine, et mitte pahandada maa-aluseid.",
      "locator": "sissejuhatus",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Membuat marah maa-alused dan maahaldjad (yang muncul dalam tradisi rakyat Estonia Selatan, Lõuna-Eesti) bisa mendatangkan penyakit dari tanah, yang juga disebut maa-alused; terutama di Saaremaa, orang tidak boleh menginjak serangga atau jalur mereka agar maa-alused tidak marah.",
        "en": "Angering the maa-alused and maahaldjad (found in South Estonian, Lõuna-Eesti, folk tradition) could bring a disease from the earth, itself also called maa-alused; mainly on Saaremaa, people were not allowed to step on insects or their paths so as not to anger the maa-alused."
      }
    }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Marțolea adalah entitas demonik dalam mitologi Rumania yang berwujud kambing berkepala manusia dan menghukum perempuan yang bekerja pada malam Selasa.",
    "en": "Marțolea is a demonic entity of Romanian mythology, shaped like a goat with a human head, that punishes women who work on Tuesday evenings.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "martolea-c01",
      "martolea-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Marțolea adalah entitas demonik dalam mitologi Rumania, terutama di Bucovina dan Maramureș, walau menurut Simeon Florea Marian ia dikenal di seluruh wilayah Rumania. Namanya juga Marț-sara atau Marți Seara, \"Selasa Malam\". Ia tinggal di pegunungan dan turun pada malam Selasa untuk memikat dengan nyanyiannya dan menghukum perempuan yang memintal wol, menjahit, merebus cucian, atau memanggang roti pada hari itu.",
      "en": "Marțolea is a demonic entity of Romanian mythology, especially in Bucovina and Maramureș, though according to Simeon Florea Marian it is known across all Romanian territory. It is also called Marț-sara or Marți Seara, \"Tuesday Evening\". It lives in the mountains and comes down on Tuesday nights to lure with its singing and punish women who spin wool, sow, boil laundry or bake bread that day.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "martolea-c01",
        "martolea-c02",
        "martolea-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Biasanya ia berwujud kambing berkepala mirip manusia dengan tanduk dan kuku, tetapi bisa menjadi perempuan tua berpakaian putih, prajurit, atau lelaki tampan sesuai siapa yang ditemuinya. Hukumannya kejam: ia melempar banyak gelendong lewat cerobong yang harus diisi benang, ditenun, dan dijahit menjadi pakaian sebelum fajar, dan yang gagal akan mati.",
      "en": "Usually it has the form of a goat with a human-like head, horns and hooves, but it can become an old woman in white, a soldier or a handsome man depending on whom it meets. Its punishment is cruel: it throws many spindles down the chimney that must be filled with thread, woven and sewn into a garment by dawn, and whoever fails dies.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "martolea-c03",
        "martolea-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Marțolea juga membalas kebaikan: perempuan yang menghormati hari Selasa mendapat telur atau bunga gunung di ambang pintu, dan sebagai pelindung 1 Maret ia memberi gadis pemakai mărțișor sekeping koin perak yang dikalungkan sepanjang tahun. Doina Ruști juga menggambarkannya sebagai kekasih jahat yang memakan jantung gadis, dan menyamakan wujud hewannya dengan Silenus, Pan, dan Mars.",
      "en": "Marțolea also rewards: women who honour Tuesday find eggs or mountain flowers at their door, and as patron of 1 March it gives girls who wear the mărțișor a silver coin worn at the neck all year. Doina Ruști also describes it as a malefic lover who eats girls' hearts, and likens its animal forms to Silenus, Pan and Mars.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "martolea-c05",
        "martolea-c07",
        "martolea-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],
```
