  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "martolea-c01",
      "source_id": "martolea-s1",
      "quote": "Marțolea is a demonic entity in Romanian mythology (especially in the regions of Bucovina and Maramureș). The entity's gender is unclear, as it can shapeshift at will. It lives up in the mountains and descends on Tuesday nights to lure with its singing and punish the women caught working.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marțolea adalah entitas demonik dalam mitologi Rumania, terutama di Bucovina dan Maramureș; jenis kelaminnya tidak jelas karena ia bisa berubah wujud sesuka hati, ia tinggal di pegunungan dan turun pada malam Selasa untuk memikat dengan nyanyiannya dan menghukum perempuan yang kedapatan bekerja.",
        "en": "Marțolea is a demonic entity of Romanian mythology, especially in Bucovina and Maramureș; its gender is unclear since it can shapeshift at will, and it lives in the mountains and comes down on Tuesday nights to lure with its singing and punish women caught working."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c02",
      "source_id": "martolea-s1",
      "quote": "Also known as Marț-sara or Marți Seara (the old Romanian words for \"Tuesday Evening\"), it is a malefic entity who demands the semi-holy day of Tuesday to be respected and who forbids four women's chores: spinning of the wool, sowing, boiling laundry and baking bread.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dikenal juga sebagai Marț-sara atau Marți Seara (\"Selasa Malam\" dalam bahasa Rumania lama), ia entitas jahat yang menuntut hari Selasa yang setengah suci dihormati dan melarang empat pekerjaan perempuan: memintal wol, menjahit, merebus cucian, dan memanggang roti.",
        "en": "Also called Marț-sara or Marți Seara (old Romanian for \"Tuesday Evening\"), it is a malefic entity that demands the semi-holy day of Tuesday be respected and forbids four women's chores: spinning wool, sowing, boiling laundry and baking bread."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c03",
      "source_id": "martolea-s1",
      "quote": "Usually its form is of a goat with a human-like head, horns and hooves. It can shapeshift into an ugly, old woman dressed all in white, a soldier or as a handsome man. To married women, it shows as an old woman; to married men as a virgin, and to unmarried women as a young charming man.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Biasanya ia berwujud kambing berkepala mirip manusia, bertanduk dan berkuku belah; ia bisa menjadi perempuan tua buruk rupa berpakaian serba putih, prajurit, atau lelaki tampan; kepada perempuan bersuami ia tampil sebagai perempuan tua, kepada lelaki beristri sebagai perawan, dan kepada perempuan lajang sebagai pemuda memikat.",
        "en": "Usually it has the form of a goat with a human-like head, horns and hooves; it can become an ugly old woman all in white, a soldier or a handsome man; to married women it appears as an old woman, to married men as a virgin, and to unmarried women as a charming young man."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c04",
      "source_id": "martolea-s2",
      "quote": "Marțolea este un personaj mitologic, răspândit pe întreg teritoriul românesc, după cum precizează Simeon Florea Marian (1847-1907), în Mitologie românească",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Simeon Florea Marian (1847–1907) dalam Mitologie românească, Marțolea adalah tokoh mitologi yang tersebar di seluruh wilayah Rumania.",
        "en": "According to Simeon Florea Marian (1847–1907) in Mitologie românească, Marțolea is a mythological figure spread across the whole Romanian territory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c05",
      "source_id": "martolea-s2",
      "quote": "Marțolea patronează serile de marți, din care cauză se mai numește și Marțsara, dar este și patronul zilei de 1 Martie, respectiv al Mărțișorului. ... Fericit că fetele îl poartă, el dăruiește un mart sau un mărțișor, adică un ban de argint curat, care se poartă la gât tot anul.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marțolea menaungi malam-malam Selasa (marți), sehingga disebut juga Marțsara, dan juga pelindung tanggal 1 Maret (Martie), yakni Mărțișor; senang melihat gadis-gadis memakainya, ia menghadiahkan mart atau mărțișor, yaitu koin perak murni yang dikalungkan sepanjang tahun.",
        "en": "Marțolea patronises Tuesday (marți) evenings, hence also the name Marțsara, and is also patron of 1 March (Martie), that is of the Mărțișor; glad to see girls wearing it, it gives a mart or mărțișor, a coin of pure silver worn at the neck all year."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c06",
      "source_id": "martolea-s2",
      "quote": "Pedeapsa administrată de Marțolea era de-a dreptul sadică: le arunca împricinatelor pe horn o puzderie de fuse, pe care bietele femei trebuiau să le umple cu lână toarsă. Din firul care rezulta trebuiau să îi țeasă o pânză și să-i facă din ea o haină până în zori. Cine nu reușea, murea.",
      "locator": "Alte variante",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hukuman dari Marțolea sungguh kejam: ia melemparkan banyak sekali gelendong lewat cerobong kepada perempuan yang bersalah, yang harus mereka isi dengan benang wol; dari benang itu mereka harus menenun kain dan menjahit pakaian untuknya sebelum fajar, dan yang gagal akan mati.",
        "en": "Marțolea's punishment was downright sadistic: it threw a host of spindles down the chimney to the guilty women, who had to fill them with spun wool, weave cloth from the thread and make it a garment by dawn; whoever failed died."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c07",
      "source_id": "martolea-s2",
      "quote": "În Dicționar de teme și simboluri de Doina Ruști, Marțolea apare și ca îndrăgostit malefic, care mănâncă inima fetelor de care se îndrăgostește. Zoomorfismele sale îl apropie de Silen, de Pan și de Marte.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Dicționar de teme și simboluri karya Doina Ruști, Marțolea juga tampil sebagai kekasih jahat yang memakan jantung gadis yang ia cintai, dan wujud hewannya mendekatkannya pada Silenus, Pan, dan Mars (Silen, Pan, Marte).",
        "en": "In Doina Ruști's Dicționar de teme și simboluri, Marțolea also appears as a malefic lover who eats the hearts of the girls it falls for, and its animal forms bring it close to Silenus, Pan and Mars (Silen, Pan, Marte)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "martolea-c08",
      "source_id": "martolea-s1",
      "quote": "Marțolea repays the women who keep the Tuesday day sacred by leaving them eggs on their doorstep or flowers from the highest mountains in Bukovina.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marțolea membalas perempuan yang menyucikan hari Selasa dengan meninggalkan telur di ambang pintu atau bunga dari pegunungan tertinggi di Bukovina.",
        "en": "Marțolea repays women who keep Tuesday holy by leaving eggs on their doorstep or flowers from the highest mountains of Bukovina."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Ora adalah roh pelindung dalam mitologi Albania yang dimiliki setiap manusia sejak lahir dan terkait nasib serta takdirnya.",
    "en": "The Ora is a protective spirit of Albanian mythology that every human has from birth, tied to their fate and destiny.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ora adalah tokoh mitologi Albania yang dimiliki setiap manusia sejak lahir, terkait nasib dan takdir, dan bertugas menjaga tatanan alam semesta. Rupanya berubah menurut watak pemiliknya: Ora putih bagi yang berani dan rajin, Ora hitam bagi yang malas atau pengecut. Di Albania Utara, Ora juga digambarkan sebagai tiga dewi yang berkumpul pada malam hari untuk menentukan nasib bayi yang baru lahir.",
      "en": "The Ora is an Albanian mythological figure that every human has from birth, tied to fate and destiny and charged with maintaining the order of the universe. In Northern Albania the Oras are also depicted as three goddesses who gather at night to determine a newborn's fate.",

        "ora-mythology-c02",

      "en": "The Ora dwell north of the Drin River, in forests, mountains, streams, lakes and caves, and in Central Albania were believed to be everywhere, fulfilling people's blessings and curses.       "author": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The Ora (Albanian: orë, definite form: ora, pl.: orë/t) is an Albanian mythological figure that every human possesses from birth, associated with human destiny and fate. The essential function of the ora is to maintain the order of the universe and to enforce its laws.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ora (bahasa Albania orë, bentuk takrif ora) adalah tokoh mitologi Albania yang dimiliki setiap manusia sejak lahir, terkait nasib dan takdir; fungsi utamanya menjaga tatanan alam semesta dan menegakkan hukumnya.",
        "en": "The Ora (Albanian orë, definite form ora) is an Albanian mythological figure that every human has from birth, tied to destiny and fate; its essential function is to maintain the order of the universe and enforce its laws."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Within Albanian folklore and poetry, the Ora is a protective spirit that every human possesses from birth. The Ora's appearance alters according to the personality and attributes of the human – “a white Ora for the brave and industrious, or a black Ora for the lazy or cowardly.”",
      "locator": "Appearance",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam folklor dan puisi Albania, Ora adalah roh pelindung yang dimiliki setiap manusia sejak lahir, dan rupanya berubah menurut watak orangnya: Ora putih bagi yang berani dan rajin, Ora hitam bagi yang malas atau pengecut.",
        "en": "In Albanian folklore and poetry the Ora is a protective spirit everyone has from birth, and its appearance changes with the person's character: a white Ora for the brave and industrious, a black Ora for the lazy or cowardly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The Ora reside towards the north of the Drin River in Northern Albania. Within Central Albania it was believed that the Ora were present everywhere, “listening to people’s blessings and curses, which they would then aim to quickly fulfil”. Outside of Central Albania Oras live in forests and mountains, residing in streams, lakes, and caves.",
      "locator": "Geographic location of the Ora",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ora berdiam di utara Sungai Drin di Albania Utara; di Albania Tengah mereka dipercaya hadir di mana-mana, mendengarkan berkat dan kutuk orang lalu berusaha cepat mewujudkannya, sedangkan di luar Albania Tengah mereka tinggal di hutan dan gunung, di sungai kecil, danau, dan gua.",
        "en": "The Ora dwell north of the Drin River in Northern Albania; in Central Albania they were believed to be everywhere, listening to people's blessings and curses and trying to fulfil them quickly, while outside Central Albania they live in forests and mountains, in streams, lakes and caves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Within Albanian folklore and poetry, Ora had the ability to take any form they pleased, including “birds, beasts, women, or serpents.” ... In Northern Albania, Oras “often appear as serpents.” – similarly to the Southern Albanian deity Vitore. Both the Ora and Vitore are “widely represented as a serpent with golden horns who brings gold.”",
      "locator": "Ability to Transform; Serpent form",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ora bisa mengambil wujud apa pun, termasuk burung, binatang buas, perempuan, atau ular; di Albania Utara mereka sering muncul sebagai ular, seperti dewi Vitore di Albania Selatan, dan keduanya digambarkan sebagai ular bertanduk emas yang membawa emas.",
        "en": "The Ora could take any form, including birds, beasts, women or serpents; in Northern Albania they often appear as serpents, like the Southern Albanian deity Vitore, and both are depicted as a golden-horned serpent that brings gold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In Northern Albania, the Oras are represented – similarly to the Southern Albanian Fatia – as a group of three mythological goddesses who gather in the night to perform the task of “determining the child’s fate at birth” and distribute their favours upon the child.",
      "locator": "The Fates",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Albania Utara, Ora digambarkan, seperti Fatia di Albania Selatan, sebagai kelompok tiga dewi yang berkumpul pada malam hari untuk menentukan nasib anak saat lahir dan membagikan anugerah mereka kepadanya.",
        "en": "In Northern Albania the Oras are represented, like the Southern Albanian Fatia, as a group of three goddesses who gather at night to determine a child's fate at birth and bestow their favours on it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Ora e maleve (e pashquar: orë, e shquar: ora, shumës: orë/t) është një figurë mitologjike shqiptare që çdo njeri posedon që nga lindja, e lidhur me fatet dhe destinimin e njeriut.",
      "locator": "hyrje",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Albania menyebutnya Ora e maleve (\"Ora pegunungan\"), tokoh mitologi Albania yang dimiliki setiap orang sejak lahir dan terkait nasib serta takdir manusia.",
        "en": "The Albanian article calls it Ora e maleve (\"Ora of the mountains\"), an Albanian mythological figure every person has from birth, tied to human fate and destiny."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ora-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "ora-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Wandering aimlessly in the mountains, the blind Zuku is met by an ora who restores his sights with some herbs and tells him to take vengeance",
      "locator": "Zuku Bajraktar",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zuku yang dibutakan dan mengembara tanpa arah di pegunungan bertemu ora yang memulihkan penglihatannya dengan ramuan dan menyuruhnya membalas dendam.",
        "en": "The blinded Zuku, wandering aimlessly in the mountains, meets an ora who restores his sight with herbs and tells him to take vengeance."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Paha adalah roh demonik dalam mitologi Finlandia, pengikut Lempo, yang dalam Kalevala membuat kapak Väinämöinen melukai lututnya.",
    "en": "Paha is a demonic spirit of Finnish mythology, a follower of Lempo, who in the Kalevala makes Väinämöinen's axe wound his knee.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "paha-c01",
      "paha-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Paha, yang namanya berarti \"kejahatan\", adalah roh demonik dalam mitologi Finlandia dan pengikut Lempo, dewa kejahatan. Dalam Kalevala, ketika Väinämöinen memahat kayu untuk membuat perahu demi gadis Pohjola, Paha bersama Hiisi dan Lempo mengganggu kapaknya: Hiisi memutar ujungnya, Lempo merenggut matanya, dan Paha menggoyang gagangnya.",
      "en": "Paha, whose name means \"evil\", is a demonic spirit of Finnish mythology and a follower of Lempo, the god of malice. In the Kalevala, when Väinämöinen hews wood to build a boat for the Maiden of Pohjola, Paha joins Hiisi and Lempo in tampering with his axe: Hiisi twists the butt, Lempo snatches the blade and Paha shakes the handle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "paha-c01",
        "paha-c05",
        "paha-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Akibatnya kapak itu membentur batu, terpental, dan melukai lutut Väinämöinen; darahnya mengalir tanpa henti sehingga ia harus berusaha keras menghentikannya. Nama tokoh ini kini juga dipakai untuk asteroid Paha, yang bersama Lempo dan Hiisi membentuk sistem asteroid rangkap tiga.",
      "en": "As a result the axe struck a stone, glanced off and wounded Väinämöinen's knee; the blood would not stop and he had to struggle to staunch it. The figure's name is now also borne by the asteroid Paha, which with Lempo and Hiisi forms a triple asteroid system.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "paha-c02",
        "paha-c07",
        "paha-c08"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "paha-c01",
      "source_id": "paha-s1",
      "quote": "Paha (finnisch; deutsch: Übel) ist ein Dämonischer Geist aus der finnischen Mythologie. Er ist ein Anhänger von Lempo, dem Gott der Bosheit.",
      "locator": "Einleitung",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Paha (bahasa Finlandia, \"kejahatan\", Übel) adalah roh demonik dalam mitologi Finlandia dan pengikut Lempo, dewa kejahatan.",
        "en": "Paha (Finnish, \"evil\", Übel) is a demonic spirit of Finnish mythology and a follower of Lempo, the god of malice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c02",
      "source_id": "paha-s1",
      "quote": "Als Väinämöinen gegen die Dämonen kämpfte, sorgte Paha zusammen mit Hiisi dafür, dass Väinämöinen sich mit seiner eigenen Axt selbst verletzte.",
      "locator": "Einleitung",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika Väinämöinen bertarung melawan para demon, Paha bersama Hiisi membuat Väinämöinen melukai dirinya sendiri dengan kapaknya.",
        "en": "When Väinämöinen fought the demons, Paha together with Hiisi made Väinämöinen wound himself with his own axe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c03",
      "source_id": "paha-s2",
      "quote": "Paha (del finès; en català: dolent) és un esperit demoníac de la mitologia finesa. És un seguidor de Lempo, el déu de la malícia.",
      "locator": "introducció",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Katalan juga menyebut Paha (bahasa Katalan dolent, \"jahat\") sebagai roh demonik mitologi Finlandia dan pengikut Lempo, dewa kedengkian.",
        "en": "The Catalan article also calls Paha (Catalan dolent, \"bad\") a demonic spirit of Finnish mythology and a follower of Lempo, the god of malice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c04",
      "source_id": "paha-s3",
      "quote": "パハ（フィンランド語: Paha）は、フィンランド神話に登場する悪霊。",
      "locator": "冒頭",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Jepang menyebut Paha sebagai roh jahat yang muncul dalam mitologi Finlandia.",
        "en": "The Japanese article describes Paha as an evil spirit appearing in Finnish mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c05",
      "source_id": "paha-s3",
      "quote": "『カレワラ』では、ワイナミョイネンがポホヨラの乙女（フィンランド語版）を手に入れるため斧で木を削って船を造っている場面（第8章151-154行目前後）で登場し、同じく悪霊のヒーシ（英語版）、レンポ（英語版） とともにワイナミョイネンに傷を負わせている。",
      "locator": "冒頭",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Kalevala, Paha muncul pada adegan Väinämöinen memahat kayu dengan kapak untuk membuat perahu demi mendapatkan gadis Pohjola (bab 8, sekitar baris 151–154), dan bersama roh jahat Hiisi dan Lempo melukai Väinämöinen.",
        "en": "In the Kalevala, Paha appears in the scene where Väinämöinen hews wood with an axe to build a boat to win the Maiden of Pohjola (chapter 8, around lines 151–154), and together with the evil spirits Hiisi and Lempo wounds Väinämöinen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c06",
      "source_id": "paha-s3",
      "quote": "Niin päivällä kolmannella Hiisi pontta pyörähytti, Lempo tempasi tereä, Paha vartta vaapahutti.",
      "locator": "冒頭",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Baris Kalevala yang dikutip berbunyi: pada hari ketiga Hiisi memutar ujung kapak, Lempo merenggut matanya, Paha menggoyang gagangnya.",
        "en": "The quoted Kalevala lines run: on the third day Hiisi twisted the axe-butt, Lempo snatched the blade, Paha shook the handle (Niin päivällä kolmannella Hiisi pontta pyörähytti, Lempo tempasi tereä, Paha vartta vaapahutti)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c07",
      "source_id": "paha-s3",
      "quote": "パハたちの悪さにより、斧が石に当たり跳ね返り、ワイナミョイネンの膝に当たって傷を負わせた。傷からは血が流れだし止まることがなく、血を止めるためワイナミョイネンは奔走することになる（第8-9章）。",
      "locator": "冒頭",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena ulah Paha dan kawan-kawannya, kapak itu membentur batu, terpental, dan melukai lutut Väinämöinen; darah mengalir tanpa henti dan Väinämöinen harus berusaha keras menghentikannya (bab 8–9).",
        "en": "Through the mischief of Paha and the others, the axe struck a stone, glanced off and wounded Väinämöinen's knee; the blood flowed without stopping and Väinämöinen had to struggle to staunch it (chapters 8–9)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "paha-c08",
      "source_id": "paha-s3",
      "quote": "小惑星パハの名前はこのパハに由来する。レンポ、ヒーシとともに三重小惑星を成している。",
      "locator": "冒頭",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama asteroid Paha berasal dari tokoh ini, dan bersama Lempo dan Hiisi asteroid itu membentuk sistem asteroid rangkap tiga.",
        "en": "The asteroid Paha is named after this figure, and together with Lempo and Hiisi it forms a triple asteroid system."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rahko adalah tokoh mitologi Finlandia yang dikaitkan dengan memudarnya Bulan, digambarkan menghitamkan atau melumuri Bulan dengan ter.",
    "en": "Rahko is a figure of Finnish mythology linked to the waning of the Moon, said to blacken it or cover it in tar.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "rahko-c01",
      "rahko-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rahko atau Rahkoi adalah tokoh mitologi Finlandia yang pertama kali disebut pada 1551 oleh Mikael Agricola sebagai sosok yang \"membelah Bulan menjadi hitam\", sehingga dikaitkan dengan memudarnya Bulan. Pada 1789 Christfried Ganander menulis bahwa orang Tavastia zaman dulu menganggapnya hantu yang menghitamkan Bulan. Di tempat lain namanya berarti mimpi buruk, nama penyakit, atau roh rumah yang jahat.",
      "en": "Rahko or Rahkoi is a figure of Finnish mythology first mentioned in 1551 by Mikael Agricola as one who \"split the Moon black\", and so linked to the waning of the Moon. In 1789 Christfried Ganander wrote that the Tavastians of old held him a ghost who blackened the Moon. Elsewhere his name means a nightmare, a disease or an evil household spirit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rahko-c01",
        "rahko-c02",
        "rahko-c03",
        "rahko-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan orang Finlandia Hutan, Rahko adalah seseorang yang mati bunuh diri dan menempa bulan baru dari kuku dan lemak sapi setelah para kapeet memakan bulan lama. Orang Sami di Finlandia mengenal Mano-Rakko, pencuri yang naik ke Bulan untuk melumurinya dengan ter lalu tersangkut di sana dengan ember dan kuas. Kaarle Krohn mengartikan kata rahko sebagai \"kulit pecah-pecah\".",
      "en": "In Forest Finnish belief, Rahko was a suicide who forged a new moon from cow's hooves and fat after the kapeet ate the old one. The Sámi in Finland knew Mano-Rakko, a thief who went up to the Moon to tar it and got stuck there with his bucket and brush. Kaarle Krohn took the word rahko to mean \"chapping\".",
      "claim_ids": [
        "rahko-c04",
        "rahko-c05",
        "rahko-c08"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "rahko-c01",
      "source_id": "rahko-s1",
      "quote": "Rahko or Rahkoi is a figure in Finnish mythology. He was first mentioned in writing in 1551 by Mikael Agricola as someone who \"split the Moon black\" (\"Rachkoi Cuun mustaxi jacoi\"), so he has been connected to the waning of the Moon.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rahko atau Rahkoi adalah tokoh mitologi Finlandia yang pertama kali disebut secara tertulis pada 1551 oleh Mikael Agricola sebagai sosok yang \"membelah Bulan menjadi hitam\" (\"Rachkoi Cuun mustaxi jacoi\"), sehingga ia dikaitkan dengan memudarnya Bulan.",
        "en": "Rahko or Rahkoi is a figure of Finnish mythology first mentioned in writing in 1551 by Mikael Agricola as one who \"split the Moon black\" (\"Rachkoi Cuun mustaxi jacoi\"), so he has been linked to the waning of the Moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c02",
      "source_id": "rahko-s1",
      "quote": "In 1789, Christfried Ganander wrote that Rahkoi is a ghost who the Tavastians of old believed blackened the Moon.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1789 Christfried Ganander menulis bahwa Rahkoi adalah hantu yang menurut kepercayaan orang Tavastia zaman dulu menghitamkan Bulan.",
        "en": "In 1789 Christfried Ganander wrote that Rahkoi is a ghost the Tavastians of old believed blackened the Moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c03",
      "source_id": "rahko-s1",
      "quote": "In South Ostrobothnia, Rahko has meant a nightmare.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Ostrobothnia Selatan, kata Rahko berarti mimpi buruk.",
        "en": "In South Ostrobothnia, Rahko has meant a nightmare."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c04",
      "source_id": "rahko-s1",
      "quote": "In Forest Finnish beliefs, kapeet ate the moon away every month and Rahko, someone who had committed suicide, forged a new moon from cow's hooves and fat. After being a Rahko for long enough, you'd become a kave, and a new Rahko took your place.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan orang Finlandia Hutan, para kapeet memakan bulan setiap bulan dan Rahko, seseorang yang mati bunuh diri, menempa bulan baru dari kuku dan lemak sapi; setelah cukup lama menjadi Rahko, ia menjadi kave dan Rahko baru menggantikannya.",
        "en": "In Forest Finnish beliefs, the kapeet ate the moon away every month and Rahko, someone who had committed suicide, forged a new moon from cow's hooves and fat; after being Rahko long enough one became a kave and a new Rahko took one's place."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c05",
      "source_id": "rahko-s1",
      "quote": "The Sámi in Finland knew a figure called Mano-Rakko (\"Rakko of the Moon\"), who had been a thief who had gone up to the Moon to cover it in tar so its light wouldn't give him away. However, he got stuck and can still be seen on the Moon, a bucket of tar in one hand and a brush in the other.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Sami di Finlandia mengenal Mano-Rakko (\"Rakko dari Bulan\"), pencuri yang naik ke Bulan untuk melumurinya dengan ter agar cahayanya tidak membongkar dirinya; tetapi ia tersangkut dan masih terlihat di Bulan, memegang ember ter dan kuas.",
        "en": "The Sámi in Finland knew Mano-Rakko (\"Rakko of the Moon\"), a thief who went up to the Moon to cover it in tar so its light would not give him away; but he got stuck and can still be seen on the Moon with a tar bucket in one hand and a brush in the other."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c06",
      "source_id": "rahko-s3",
      "quote": "Rahko conosciuto anche come Raako nella mitologia finlandese ed in quella careliana è considerato una divinità del tempo e del declino delle fasi della luna",
      "locator": "introduzione",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Italia menyebut Rahko, juga dikenal sebagai Raako, dalam mitologi Finlandia dan Karelia (careliana) sebagai dewa waktu dan memudarnya fase bulan.",
        "en": "The Italian article says Rahko, also known as Raako, is regarded in Finnish and Karelian (careliana) mythology as a deity of time and of the waning phases of the moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c07",
      "source_id": "rahko-s2",
      "quote": "Rahko esiintyy myös taudin nimenä ja pahana kodinhaltijana.",
      "locator": "johdanto",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rahko juga muncul sebagai nama penyakit dan sebagai roh rumah (kodinhaltija) yang jahat.",
        "en": "Rahko also appears as the name of a disease and as an evil household spirit (kodinhaltija)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rahko-c08",
      "source_id": "rahko-s1",
      "quote": "Kaarle Krohn believed the word rahko means \"chapping\". The Veps people have a similar being who appears as a riihi haltija (rihenrahkoi) or an oven haltija (päčinrahkoi).",
      "locator": "Name",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaarle Krohn berpendapat kata rahko berarti \"kulit pecah-pecah\"; orang Veps punya makhluk serupa yang muncul sebagai haltija lumbung pengering (rihenrahkoi) atau haltija tungku (päčinrahkoi).",
        "en": "Kaarle Krohn believed the word rahko means \"chapping\"; the Veps have a similar being appearing as a drying-barn haltija (rihenrahkoi) or an oven haltija (päčinrahkoi)."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Vampir Richmond adalah legenda urban dari Richmond, Virginia, tentang vampir yang konon bersemayam di mausoleum W. W. Pool di Pemakaman Hollywood.",
    "en": "The Richmond Vampire is an urban legend from Richmond, Virginia, about a vampire said to lie in the W. W. Pool mausoleum in Hollywood Cemetery.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vampir Richmond, yang secara lokal juga disebut Vampir Hollywood, adalah legenda urban dari Richmond, Virginia. Warga mengklaim mausoleum W. W. Pool di Pemakaman Hollywood menyimpan jasad vampir yang konon diusir dari Inggris pada abad ke-19. Legenda lisan ini beredar sejak 1960-an, mungkin dipicu arsitektur makam bergaya Mason dan Mesir kuno dengan dua huruf W yang mirip taring, dan populer di kalangan mahasiswa Virginia Commonwealth University di sebelahnya.",
      "en": "The Richmond Vampire, locally also called the Hollywood Vampire, is an urban legend from Richmond, Virginia. Residents claim the W. W. Pool mausoleum in Hollywood Cemetery holds the remains of a vampire supposedly driven out of England in the 19th century. The oral legend circulated by the 1960s, perhaps inspired by the tomb's Masonic and ancient Egyptian architecture with double Ws resembling fangs, and became popular among students of the neighbouring Virginia Commonwealth University.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "richmond-vampire-c01",
        "richmond-vampire-c02",
        "richmond-vampire-c03"

      "en": "Since 2001 the story has been combined with the 1925 collapse of the Church Hill Tunnel: supposedly the digging woke a blood-covered, jagged-toothed creature found crouching over a victim.       "id": "richmond-vampire-c01",
      "source_id": "richmond-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "The Richmond Vampire (also called locally the Hollywood Vampire) is an urban legend from Richmond, Virginia.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vampir Richmond (secara lokal juga disebut Vampir Hollywood) adalah legenda urban dari Richmond, Virginia.",
        "en": "The Richmond Vampire (locally also called the Hollywood Vampire) is an urban legend from Richmond, Virginia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "richmond-vampire-c02",
      "source_id": "richmond-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Local residents claim that the mausoleum of W. W. Pool (Dated 1913) in Hollywood Cemetery holds the remains of a vampire. Supposedly Pool was run out of England in the 19th century for being a vampire. Oral legends to this effect were circulating by the 1960s.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Warga setempat mengklaim bahwa mausoleum W. W. Pool (bertarikh 1913) di Pemakaman Hollywood menyimpan jasad vampir; konon Pool diusir dari Inggris pada abad ke-19 karena vampir, dan legenda lisan semacam ini sudah beredar pada 1960-an.",
        "en": "Local residents claim that the mausoleum of W. W. Pool (dated 1913) in Hollywood Cemetery holds a vampire's remains; supposedly Pool was driven out of England in the 19th century for being a vampire, and oral legends to this effect circulated by the 1960s."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "richmond-vampire-c03",
      "source_id": "richmond-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "They may be influenced by the architecture of the tomb, which has both Masonic and ancient Egyptian elements, and double Ws looking like fangs. Because this cemetery is adjacent to Virginia Commonwealth University, the story became popular among students, especially from the 1980s onward.       "locator": "lead",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini mungkin dipengaruhi arsitektur makam yang memiliki unsur Mason dan Mesir kuno serta dua huruf W yang mirip taring; karena pemakaman itu bersebelahan dengan Virginia Commonwealth University, ceritanya populer di kalangan mahasiswa terutama sejak 1980-an, dan pertama kali dicetak di koran mahasiswa Commonwealth Times pada 1976.",
        "en": "The legends may be influenced by the tomb's architecture, with Masonic and ancient Egyptian elements and double Ws resembling fangs; because the cemetery adjoins Virginia Commonwealth University, the story became popular among students especially from the 1980s, and it first appeared in print in the student newspaper Commonwealth Times in 1976."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "richmond-vampire-c04",
      "source_id": "richmond-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "According to this newer story, the tunneling awakened an ancient evil that lived under Church Hill and brought the tunnel crashing down on the workers. Rescue teams found an unearthly blood-covered creature with jagged teeth and skin hanging from its muscular body crouching over one of the victims.",
      "locator": "The Church Hill Tunnel",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut versi yang lebih baru, penggalian terowongan membangunkan kejahatan purba yang hidup di bawah Church Hill dan meruntuhkan terowongan itu menimpa para pekerja; tim penyelamat menemukan makhluk berlumur darah bergigi bergerigi dengan kulit bergelantungan dari tubuh berotot, berjongkok di atas salah satu korban.",
        "en": "In this newer version, the tunnelling awakened an ancient evil living under Church Hill that brought the tunnel down on the workers; rescuers found an unearthly blood-covered creature with jagged teeth and skin hanging from its muscular body crouching over a victim."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "richmond-vampire-c05",
      "source_id": "richmond-vampire-s1",
      "quote": "Since 2001, the vampire story has been combined with the collapse of the Chesapeake and Ohio Railroad's Church Hill Tunnel under Church Hill, a neighborhood of eastern Richmond, Virginia, which buried several workers on Friday, October 2, 1925. This part of the story did not show up online until 2001 and was first reported in print in 2007",
      "locator": "The Church Hill Tunnel",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejak 2001, cerita vampir ini digabungkan dengan runtuhnya Terowongan Church Hill milik Chesapeake and Ohio Railroad di bawah Church Hill, Richmond timur, yang mengubur beberapa pekerja pada Jumat, 2 Oktober 1925; bagian cerita ini baru muncul daring pada 2001 dan pertama dicetak pada 2007.",
        "en": "Since 2001 the vampire story has been combined with the collapse of the Chesapeake and Ohio Railroad's Church Hill Tunnel under Church Hill in eastern Richmond, which buried several workers on Friday, October 2, 1925; this part appeared online only in 2001 and in print first in 2007."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "richmond-vampire-c06",
      "source_id": "richmond-vampire-s2",
      "quote": "El vampiro de Richmond hace referencia a una leyenda urbana de la ciudad homónima, capital del estado de Virginia (Estados Unidos).",
      "locator": "introducción",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Spanyol menyebut Vampir Richmond sebagai legenda urban dari kota Richmond, ibu kota negara bagian Virginia, Amerika Serikat (Estados Unidos).",
        "en": "The Spanish article describes the Richmond Vampire as an urban legend of the city of Richmond, capital of the state of Virginia, United States (Estados Unidos)."

    {

      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut penjelasan ini, \"makhluk\" yang lolos dari runtuhnya terowongan sebenarnya adalah juru api kereta berusia 28 tahun, Benjamin F. Mosby (1896–1925), yang sedang menyekop batu bara ke tungku lokomotif uap tanpa baju ketika runtuhan terjadi dan ketelnya pecah.",
        "en": "In this explanation the \"creature\" that escaped the collapse was actually the 28-year-old railroad fireman Benjamin F. Mosby (1896–1925), who had been shovelling coal into a steam locomotive's firebox shirtless when the cave-in occurred and the boiler burst."

    "id": "Rokita adalah iblis dalam cerita rakyat Polandia yang mendiami rawa, hutan, dan pohon dedalu tua, sangat kuat tetapi tidak terlalu cerdik.",
    "en": "Rokita is a devil of Polish folklore dwelling in bogs, forests and old willow trees, very strong but not very cunning.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rokita adalah iblis dalam cerita rakyat Polandia yang konon mendiami gerbang ke dunia bawah: lahan basah, hutan, dan bagian dalam pohon dedalu tua. Ia sangat kuat tetapi tidak terlalu cerdik. Di Łęczyca ia sering disamakan dengan Boruta dan digambarkan sebagai jenderal bermantel emas, serta bisa muncul sebagai babi, burung, ikan, kuda, anjing, tikus, atau kucing, selalu dengan niat jahat terhadap manusia.",
      "en": "Rokita is a devil of Polish folklore said to inhabit gateways to the underworld: wetlands, forests and the insides of old willow trees. He is enormously strong but not very cunning.       "quote": "Rokita ( Polish: [rɔˈki.ta] ) is a demon in Polish folklore, that is said to inhabit the gateways to the underworld, including the wetlands, forests, and the insides of old willow trees. In some local tales, he is seen as an equivalent of Leshy.",

      "quote": "Rokita este un diavol în folclorul polonez despre care se spune că locuiește în zone asociate cu poarta către lumea cealaltă",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rumania juga menyebut Rokita sebagai iblis dalam folklor Polandia yang tinggal di tempat-tempat yang dikaitkan dengan gerbang ke dunia lain.",

      "id": "rokita-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "rokita-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "Rokita – imię jednego z diabłów, występującego w polskich podaniach ludowych. Cechuje go niewyobrażalna siła, ale za to nie jest zbyt przebiegły.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",

        "id": "Rokita adalah nama salah satu iblis dalam legenda rakyat Polandia; ia memiliki kekuatan luar biasa tetapi tidak terlalu cerdik.",

    {
      "id": "rokita-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "rokita-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "W przedstawieniach łęczyckich Rokitę często traktują jako ekwiwalent Boruty, przedstawiają jako generała w złotym płaszczu bądź przyodziany w kapelusz oraz surdut. ... ukazuje się pod postaciami zwierzęcymi, takimi jak: świnia, ptak, ryba, koń, pies, mysz. Niezależnie od przybranej postaci czy to ludzkiej czy zwierzęcej cechują go wrogie intencje w stosunku do ludzi.",
      "locator": "Nazwy regionalne",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam penggambaran dari Łęczyca, Rokita sering disamakan dengan Boruta dan digambarkan sebagai jenderal bermantel emas atau bertopi dan berjas panjang; di sana juga dipercaya ia muncul sebagai babi, burung, ikan, kuda, anjing, atau tikus, dan dalam wujud apa pun ia berniat jahat terhadap manusia.",
        "en": "In Łęczyca depictions Rokita is often treated as an equivalent of Boruta, shown as a general in a golden cloak or wearing a hat and frock coat; there it was also believed he appears as a pig, bird, fish, horse, dog or mouse, and whatever his form he is hostile to people."
      }

    {
      "id": "rokita-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "rokita-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "Według innej wersji legendy Rokita, zwany inaczej Rokietnikiem, był złą duszą zamieszkującą bagna (stąd jego nazwa pochodząca od mchu rokietnika często występującego w środkowoeuropejskich lasach) i topiącą tych, którzy wdarli się na teren jego siedliszcza.",
      "locator": "Legendy",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut versi legenda lain, Rokita yang juga disebut Rokietnik adalah jiwa jahat penghuni rawa (namanya dari lumut rokietnik yang umum di hutan Eropa Tengah) yang menenggelamkan orang yang masuk ke wilayahnya.",
        "en": "In another version of the legend, Rokita, also called Rokietnik, was an evil soul dwelling in bogs (his name coming from rokietnik moss, common in Central European forests) who drowned those who intruded on his territory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rokita-folklore-c06",
      "source_id": "rokita-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "Kolejna z legend mówi, że Rokita razem z Borutą udali się do karczmy znajdującej się w Łodzi, aby napić się tamtejszego piwa.",
      "locator": "Legendy",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda lain bercerita bahwa Rokita bersama Boruta pergi ke sebuah kedai di Łódź untuk minum bir setempat.",
        "en": "Another legend tells that Rokita and Boruta went to an inn in Łódź to drink the local beer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rokita-folklore-c07",
      "source_id": "rokita-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "Wierzono, że potrafi przybierać postać kota. Czarownice w zaklęciach ludowych prosiły go szczególnie o obfitość krów, aby dawały więcej mleka.",
      "locator": "Wierzenia ludowe",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rokita dipercaya bisa berwujud kucing, dan para penyihir dalam mantra rakyat terutama memintanya agar sapi-sapi memberi lebih banyak susu.",
        "en": "Rokita was believed able to take the form of a cat, and witches in folk spells asked him especially for abundant cows giving more milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rokita-folklore-c08",
      "source_id": "rokita-folklore-s3",
      "quote": "Gdy tak zawodził i płakał, stanął przed nim Rokita. ... Rokita ofiarował mu dużo pieniędzy, bez żadnego oprocentowania. Z warunkiem, że w ustalonym dniu oraz godzinie odda mu sumę co do grosza, w innym wypadku zabierze jego duszę.",
      "locator": "Legendy",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebuah legenda, ketika seorang petani miskin meratap dan menangis, Rokita muncul di hadapannya dan menawarkan banyak uang tanpa bunga, dengan syarat dikembalikan utuh pada hari dan jam yang ditentukan, kalau tidak jiwanya akan diambil.",
        "en": "In one legend, as a poor peasant wailed and wept, Rokita appeared before him and offered a great deal of money without interest, on condition it be repaid to the penny at a set day and hour, or else he would take his soul."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

    },
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

    "id": "Samca adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Rumania yang menakuti anak kecil dan perempuan yang akan melahirkan, dan bisa ditangkal dengan menulis ke-19 namanya.",
    "en": "Samca is an evil spirit of Romanian mythology who frightens small children and women about to give birth, and can be warded off by writing her 19 names.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Samca adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Rumania. Ia paling sering tampil sebagai perempuan telanjang berambut kusut hingga tumit, bertangan besi dengan kuku setajam jarum rajut, dan berlidah api, tetapi bisa juga menjadi babi ganas, anjing menyeringai, kucing tak berbulu, gagak bermata darah, atau laba-laba hitam besar. Dalam beberapa legenda ia juga muncul dalam mimpi pengantin pria muda untuk menggoda mereka.",
      "en": "Samca is an evil spirit of Romanian mythology. She most often appears as a naked woman with dishevelled hair to her heels, iron hands with nails sharp as knitting needles and a tongue of fire, but can also become a fierce pig, a grinning dog, a hairless cat, a bloody-eyed crow or a big black spider. In some legends she also appears in the dreams of young bridegrooms to tempt them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "samca-c01",
        "samca-c02",
        "samca-c06",
        "samca-c07"

    {
      "id": "Sekitar bulan purnama pada akhir bulan, Samca menampakkan diri kepada anak di bawah empat tahun hingga mereka jatuh sakit, dan kepada perempuan yang akan melahirkan. Untuk melindungi diri, orang menulis ke-19 namanya di dinding rumah. Pada 1900 Rabbi Moses Gaster mencatat kemiripan jimat-jimat semacam ini di Balkan dan menyebut Samca sebagai Avezuha, \"sayap Setan\".",
      "en": "Around the full moon at the month's end, Samca appears to children under four, who fall ill, and to women about to give birth.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "samca-c01",
      "source_id": "samca-s1",
      "quote": "Samca is an evil spirit in Romanian mythology. She most commonly takes the appearance of a naked woman with disheveled hair growing down to her heels, with small eyes that shine as brightly as the stars, with iron hands and long nails sharp as knitting needles or hooked as sickles and a tongue of fire.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Samca adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Rumania yang paling sering tampil sebagai perempuan telanjang berambut kusut hingga tumit, bermata kecil berkilau seperti bintang, bertangan besi dengan kuku panjang setajam jarum rajut atau melengkung seperti sabit, dan berlidah api.",
        "en": "Samca is an evil spirit of Romanian mythology who most often appears as a naked woman with dishevelled hair down to her heels, small eyes shining like stars, iron hands with long nails sharp as knitting needles or hooked like sickles, and a tongue of fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c02",
      "source_id": "samca-s1",
      "quote": "Other forms Samca can take are: a very large and fierce pig, a grinning dog showing awful teeth, a hairless cat with fiery, bulging eyes, a crow with bloody eyes, and a big black spider.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Samca juga bisa berwujud babi besar yang ganas, anjing menyeringai dengan gigi mengerikan, kucing tak berbulu bermata menyala dan melotot, gagak bermata darah, dan laba-laba hitam besar.",
        "en": "Samca can also take the form of a huge fierce pig, a grinning dog with awful teeth, a hairless cat with fiery bulging eyes, a crow with bloody eyes and a big black spider."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c03",
      "source_id": "samca-s1",
      "quote": "It can come out at the end of each month, around full moon, and it usually appears to children under the age of four, who are so frightened that they become sick immediately. The disease children develop after Samca appears to them is called \"the children's malice\". This demon can also appear to women who are about to give birth.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bisa keluar pada akhir setiap bulan, sekitar bulan purnama, biasanya menampakkan diri kepada anak di bawah empat tahun yang begitu ketakutan hingga langsung sakit; penyakit itu disebut \"kedengkian anak-anak\"; demon ini juga muncul kepada perempuan yang akan melahirkan.",
        "en": "It may come out at the end of each month, around full moon, usually appearing to children under four, who are so frightened they fall ill at once; the illness is called \"the children's malice\"; the demon can also appear to women about to give birth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c04",
      "source_id": "samca-s1",
      "quote": "Samca has 19 names: Vestitia, Navadaraia, Valnomia, Sina, Nicosda, Avezuha, Scorcoila, Tiha, Miha, Grompa, Slalo, Necauza, Hatavu, Hulila, Huva, Ghiana, Gluviana, Prava, and Samca. To defend against Samca, people need to write all her 19 names on a wall of the house",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Samca punya 19 nama: Vestitia, Navadaraia, Valnomia, Sina, Nicosda, Avezuha, Scorcoila, Tiha, Miha, Grompa, Slalo, Necauza, Hatavu, Hulila, Huva, Ghiana, Gluviana, Prava, dan Samca; untuk melindungi diri, orang harus menulis ke-19 nama itu di dinding rumah.",
        "en": "Samca has 19 names: Vestitia, Navadaraia, Valnomia, Sina, Nicosda, Avezuha, Scorcoila, Tiha, Miha, Grompa, Slalo, Necauza, Hatavu, Hulila, Huva, Ghiana, Gluviana, Prava and Samca; to protect themselves people must write all 19 names on a wall of the house."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c05",
      "source_id": "samca-s1",
      "quote": "The Samca was described in 1900 by Rabbi Moses Gaster, who noted the similarities between Christian (namely Romanian, Greek, and Slavonic) and Semitic (Aramaic) charms against child-stealing, and the frequency of these tales, both oral and written, in the Balkans. Per a folk incantation recorded from \"the mouth of the peasants of Roumania\", Gaster described Samca as Avezuha, the \"wing of Satan\".",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Samca dideskripsikan pada 1900 oleh Rabbi Moses Gaster, yang mencatat kemiripan jimat Kristen (Rumania, Yunani, Slavonik) dan Semit (Aram) melawan pencurian anak serta seringnya kisah ini di Balkan; berdasarkan mantra rakyat dari \"mulut para petani Rumania\", Gaster menyebut Samca sebagai Avezuha, \"sayap Setan\".",
        "en": "Samca was described in 1900 by Rabbi Moses Gaster, who noted the similarity of Christian (Romanian, Greek, Slavonic) and Semitic (Aramaic) charms against child-stealing and how common these tales were in the Balkans; from a folk incantation recorded \"from the mouth of the peasants of Roumania\", Gaster called Samca Avezuha, the \"wing of Satan\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c06",
      "source_id": "samca-s2",
      "quote": "Samca este un personaj al legendelor românești, un spirit necurat, foarte urât și fioros",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rumania menyebut Samca sebagai tokoh legenda Rumania, roh najis yang sangat buruk rupa dan ganas.",
        "en": "The Romanian article calls Samca a figure of Romanian legends, an unclean spirit, very ugly and fierce."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c07",
      "source_id": "samca-s2",
      "quote": "În tradiție ea este de asemenea asociată în mai multe legende cu Sfârșitul Lumii, cu hedonismul, sexul și plăcerile carnale. Ea le apărea în vis tinerilor miri în încercarea de a-i face să păcătuiască și să își însele proaspetele neveste.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi ia juga dikaitkan dalam beberapa legenda dengan Akhir Dunia, hedonisme, seks, dan kenikmatan jasmani, dan muncul dalam mimpi para pengantin pria muda untuk membujuk mereka berdosa dan mengkhianati istri baru mereka.",
        "en": "In tradition she is also linked in several legends with the End of the World, hedonism, sex and carnal pleasures, appearing in the dreams of young bridegrooms to tempt them to sin and betray their new wives."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "samca-c08",
      "source_id": "samca-s1",
      "quote": "In 2022, Hamilton Fringe Festival's new play contest was won by playwrights Natalia Bushnik and Kathleen Welch for their play, Samca, which used the mythology of Samca attacking pregnant women as a metaphor for abortion.",
      "locator": "In modern culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 2022 lomba naskah baru Hamilton Fringe Festival dimenangkan Natalia Bushnik dan Kathleen Welch lewat drama Samca, yang memakai mitos Samca menyerang perempuan hamil sebagai metafora aborsi.",
        "en": "In 2022 the Hamilton Fringe Festival new play contest was won by Natalia Bushnik and Kathleen Welch with their play Samca, which used the myth of Samca attacking pregnant women as a metaphor for abortion."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sântoaderi adalah sekelompok makhluk gaib cerita rakyat Rumania berwujud pemuda berkuku kuda yang datang ke desa dengan nyanyian dan genderang, membawa penyakit dan menculik orang yang berada di luar rumah.",
    "en": "The Sântoaderi are a group of supernatural beings of Romanian folklore, hoofed young men who come to villages with song and drums, bringing illness and abducting anyone caught outdoors.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "santoaderi-c01",
      "santoaderi-c02",
      "santoaderi-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, Sântoaderi adalah rombongan tujuh atau sembilan pemuda berkaki panjang dan berkuku kuda yang mengenakan jubah. Mereka datang secara misterius ke desa, bernyanyi dan menabuh genderang, lalu membuat orang sakit dengan membelitnya dengan rantai hingga terkena rematik atau menginjak-injak tubuhnya.",
      "en": "In Romanian folklore the Sântoaderi are a band of seven or nine long-footed, hoofed young men wearing capes. They arrive mysteriously in a village, singing and beating drums, and make people ill by wrapping them in chains, causing rheumatism, or by stamping on their bodies.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "santoaderi-c01",
        "santoaderi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Orang yang mendengar musik dan derap kuku mereka dianjurkan tetap di dalam rumah, karena di sana mereka aman. Orang yang tertinggal di luar bisa diculik dan dibawa ikut arak-arakan; sebagian kembali sehat dengan membawa hadiah, sebagian lagi kembali sakit, sekarat, atau gila.",
      "en": "People who heard their music and hoofbeats were advised to stay indoors, where they were safe. Anyone left outside could be abducted into the procession; some returned healthy with gifts, others sick, dying or insane.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "santoaderi-c03",
        "santoaderi-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya berasal dari sebutan daerah untuk Santo Theodorus, dan mereka dipercaya lebih sering muncul pada hari rayanya. Mircea Eliade membandingkan Sântoaderi dengan zâne, yang juga berkelana pada malam hari dalam arak-arakan penari, dan konon pada hari ke-24 setelah Paskah kedua kelompok itu bertemu untuk bermain dan bertukar karangan bunga.",
      "en": "Their name derives from a regional name for Saint Theodore, and they were thought most likely to appear on his feast day. Mircea Eliade compared the Sântoaderi with the zâne, who also travel by night in a procession of dancers, and on the 24th day after Easter the two groups are said to meet to play and exchange bouquets.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "santoaderi-c05",
        "santoaderi-c08",
        "santoaderi-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "sources": [

    {
      "id": "santoaderi-s2",
      "url": "https://ro.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%C3%A2ntoaderi",
      "title": "Sântoaderi",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ro)",
      "published": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c01",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s1",
      "quote": "The Sântoaderi were a group of supernatural entities found in Romanian folklore. Viewed as either seven or nine young men with long feet and hooves, they were also thought of as wearing capes.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sântoaderi adalah sekelompok makhluk gaib dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, dibayangkan sebagai tujuh atau sembilan pemuda berkaki panjang dan berkuku kuda (hooves), yang juga dianggap mengenakan jubah.",
        "en": "The Sântoaderi were a group of supernatural beings of Romanian folklore, pictured as seven or nine young men with long feet and hooves, also thought to wear capes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c02",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s1",
      "quote": "It was believed that they would mysteriously appear in a village, where they would sing, beat their drums, and cause illness for people; wrapping them up in chains causing rheumatism or stamping on their bodies.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Diyakini mereka muncul secara misterius di sebuah desa, bernyanyi, menabuh genderang, dan membuat orang sakit: membelit mereka dengan rantai sehingga terkena rematik, atau menginjak-injak tubuh mereka.",
        "en": "They were believed to appear mysteriously in a village, singing and beating drums, and to cause illness: wrapping people in chains, causing rheumatism, or stamping on their bodies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c03",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s1",
      "quote": "Upon hearing their music and the sound of hooves, people were advised to stay indoors, which was considered safe. Those that did not retreat indoors, could be kidnapped by the Sântoaderi and made part of the procession.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Begitu mendengar musik dan derap kuku mereka, orang dianjurkan tetap di dalam rumah, yang dianggap aman; yang tidak masuk rumah bisa diculik Sântoaderi dan dijadikan bagian dari arak-arakan mereka.",
        "en": "On hearing their music and hoofbeats, people were advised to stay indoors, which was thought safe; those who did not could be kidnapped by the Sântoaderi and made part of their procession."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c04",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s1",
      "quote": "The victims of such kidnappings would sometimes return in healthy condition and carrying gifts; other times, they would return sick, dying, or insane.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korban penculikan itu kadang kembali dalam keadaan sehat sambil membawa hadiah, kadang kembali dalam keadaan sakit, sekarat, atau gila.",
        "en": "Victims of such kidnappings sometimes came back healthy and carrying gifts, and at other times sick, dying or insane."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c05",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Sântoader\" derives from a regional name for Saint Theodore, with folklore suggesting that the creatures were more likely to appear on his feast day.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama \"Sântoader\" berasal dari sebutan daerah untuk Santo Theodorus, dan menurut cerita rakyat makhluk ini lebih mungkin muncul pada hari rayanya.",
        "en": "The name \"Sântoader\" derives from a regional name for Saint Theodore, and folklore holds that the creatures were more likely to appear on his feast day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c06",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s1",
      "quote": "The Sântoaderi share some similarities with the fairies of Irish folklore. The Romanian historian Mircea Eliade also noted a similarity between the Sântoaderi and the zîne, the Romanian equivalent of the fairy godmother",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sântoaderi memiliki beberapa kemiripan dengan peri dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia; sejarawan Rumania Mircea Eliade juga mencatat kemiripan antara Sântoaderi dan zîne, padanan Rumania untuk ibu peri.",
        "en": "The Sântoaderi share some similarities with the fairies of Irish folklore; the Romanian historian Mircea Eliade also noted a resemblance between the Sântoaderi and the zîne, the Romanian equivalent of the fairy godmother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c07",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s2",
      "quote": "Sântoaderii sunt un grup de entități supranaturale din folclorul românesc.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rumania menyebut Sântoaderi sebagai sekelompok entitas gaib dalam folklor Rumania.",
        "en": "The Romanian article describes the Sântoaderi as a group of supernatural entities in Romanian folklore."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c08",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s2",
      "quote": "Există, de asemenea, credința că în a 24-a zi după Paște, zânele și Sântoaderii se întâlnesc pentru a se juca și a-și oferi buchete de flori.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada pula kepercayaan bahwa pada hari ke-24 setelah Paskah (a 24-a zi după Paște), zânele și Sântoaderii, yaitu para zâne dan Sântoaderi, bertemu untuk bermain dan saling memberi karangan bunga.",
        "en": "There is also a belief that on the 24th day after Easter (a 24-a zi după Paște) the zânele și Sântoaderii, that is the zâne and the Sântoaderi, meet to play and give each other bouquets of flowers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "santoaderi-c09",
      "source_id": "santoaderi-s2",
      "quote": "Istoricul român al religiilor Mircea Eliade a remarcat o asemănare între Sântoaderi și zâne, despre care se credea că, de asemenea, călătoresc noaptea într-un cortegiu de dansatoare.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejarawan agama Rumania Mircea Eliade mencatat kemiripan antara Sântoaderi dan zâne, yang juga dipercaya berkelana pada malam hari dalam arak-arakan penari.",
        "en": "The Romanian historian of religions Mircea Eliade noted a resemblance between the Sântoaderi and the zâne, who were also believed to travel by night in a procession of dancers."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tsar Laut adalah raja alam laut dalam dongeng dan bylina Slavia Timur, penguasa semua perairan yang tinggal di istana bawah air dan gemar menari mengikuti musik, terkenal dari kisah Sadko dan Vasilisa yang Bijak.",
    "en": "The Sea Tsar is the king of the sea realms in East Slavic folktales and bylinas, lord of all waters who lives in an underwater palace and loves to dance to music, best known from the tales of Sadko and Vasilisa the Wise.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sea-tsar-c01",
      "sea-tsar-c06",
      "sea-tsar-c08"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tsar Laut adalah tokoh dongeng dan bylina Slavia Timur yang memerintah alam laut. Ia adalah tuan dasar laut, penguasa semua perairan dan para penghuni bawah air, serta pemilik harta tak terhitung. Istananya terbuat dari kristal, perak, emas, dan batu permata, dan letaknya berganti-ganti antara laut biru, sebuah pulau, dan Danau Ilmen.",
      "en": "The Sea Tsar is a character of East Slavic folktales and bylinas who rules the sea realms. He is master of the sea floor, lord of all waters and their underwater dwellers, and owner of countless treasures. His palace is made of crystal, silver, gold and precious stones, and its location shifts between the blue sea, an island and Lake Ilmen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sea-tsar-c01",
        "sea-tsar-c06",
        "sea-tsar-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia gemar bersenang-senang: musisi yang tenggelam dipanggil ke istananya untuk bermain, dan ketika ia menari, laut bergolak dan badai pun datang. Dalam bylina tentang Sadko, ia mula-mula membuat Sadko kaya, lalu menuntut Sadko turun ke kerajaannya. Dalam dongeng Vasilisa yang Bijak, ia ingin menahan Ivan-tsarevich, tetapi putrinya Vasilisa kabur bersama sang pangeran dan menyelamatkannya dari kejaran ayahnya.",
      "en": "He loves merrymaking: drowned musicians are summoned to his palace to play, and when he dances the sea storms and a tempest rises. In the bylina about Sadko he first makes Sadko rich, then demands that Sadko come down to his kingdom. In the tale of Vasilisa the Wise he wants to keep Ivan-tsarevich, but his daughter Vasilisa escapes with the prince and saves him from her father's pursuit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sea-tsar-c07",
        "sea-tsar-c08",
        "sea-tsar-c09",
        "sea-tsar-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tsar Laut perlu dibedakan dari roh air vodyanoy. Vodyanoy dalam kepercayaan rakyat adalah makhluk berperut gembung yang tinggal di lubuk dekat penggilingan dan menenggelamkan orang, sedangkan Tsar Laut tinggal di istana mewah dan mencintai musik. Vsevolod Miller menduga gambarannya dipengaruhi dewa laut Finlandia Ahti dan meragukan bahwa ia mencerminkan kepercayaan pagan Slavia Timur kuno; sebagian orang juga menyejajarkannya dengan morski kralj Slavia Selatan.",
      "en": "The Sea Tsar should be distinguished from the water spirit vodyanoy. The vodyanoy of folk belief is a bloated creature living in pools near mills and drowning people, whereas the Sea Tsar lives in rich halls and loves music. Vsevolod Miller suspected that his image was influenced by the Finnish sea god Ahti and doubted that he reflects old East Slavic pagan belief; some also compare him with the South Slavic morski kralj.",

  "claims": [

      "id": "sea-tsar-c01",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s1",
      "quote": "is a character in East Slavic folktales and bylinas, the tsar of the sea realms. The best known examples are bylinas about Sadko and fairy tales about Vasilisa the Wise, such as The Sea Tsar and Vasilisa the Wise.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tsar Laut adalah tokoh dalam dongeng dan bylina (epos lisan) Slavia Timur, raja alam laut; contoh paling terkenal adalah bylina tentang Sadko dan dongeng tentang Vasilisa yang Bijak, seperti The Sea Tsar and Vasilisa the Wise.",
        "en": "The Sea Tsar is a character of East Slavic folktales and bylinas (oral epics), the tsar of the sea realms; the best-known examples are the bylinas about Sadko and the fairy tales about Vasilisa the Wise, such as The Sea Tsar and Vasilisa the Wise."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c02",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s1",
      "quote": "Vsevolod Miller suggested that the image of the Sea Tsar was influenced by the Finnish god Ahti, known for the pomors (Russian seashore dwellers of the Russian North).",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vsevolod Miller berpendapat bahwa gambaran Tsar Laut dipengaruhi dewa Finlandia Ahti, yang dikenal oleh kaum pomor (penghuni pesisir di Rusia Utara).",
        "en": "Vsevolod Miller suggested that the image of the Sea Tsar was influenced by the Finnish god Ahti, known to the pomors (seashore dwellers of the Russian North)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c03",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s1",
      "quote": "Also Miller doubted that the image of Sea Tsar in these fairy tales reflects old East Slavic pagan beliefs and is probably based on the tales of itinerary storytellers.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Miller juga meragukan bahwa gambaran Tsar Laut dalam dongeng-dongeng ini mencerminkan kepercayaan pagan Slavia Timur kuno; menurutnya gambaran itu mungkin berasal dari kisah para pencerita keliling.",
        "en": "Miller also doubted that the Sea Tsar of these tales reflects old East Slavic pagan belief, and thought it was probably based on the tales of itinerant storytellers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c04",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s1",
      "quote": "The Sea Tsar should not be confused with the water spirit, vodyanoy.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tsar Laut tidak boleh dikacaukan dengan roh air vodyanoy.",
        "en": "The Sea Tsar should not be confused with the water spirit vodyanoy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c05",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s1",
      "quote": "Some draw the parallel of the Sea Tsar with the tale about South Slavic morski kralj recorded, e.g., by Slovenian poet and ethnologist Matija Valjavec",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang menyejajarkan Tsar Laut dengan kisah morski kralj (raja laut) Slavia Selatan yang dicatat antara lain oleh penyair dan etnolog Slovenia Matija Valjavec.",
        "en": "Some draw a parallel between the Sea Tsar and the tale of the South Slavic morski kralj (sea king) recorded, for example, by the Slovenian poet and ethnologist Matija Valjavec."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c06",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s2",
      "quote": "персонаж русских народных сказок и былин: хозяин дна морского, повелитель всех вод, владыка над подводными обитателями, владелец несметных сокровищ.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rusia menyebutnya tokoh dongeng dan bylina rakyat Rusia: tuan dasar laut, penguasa semua perairan, raja para penghuni bawah air, dan pemilik harta tak terhitung; ia juga disebut Царь Водяник, Поддонный царь, dan Заморский царь.",
        "en": "The Russian article calls him a character of Russian folktales and bylinas: master of the sea floor, lord of all waters, ruler of the underwater dwellers and owner of countless treasures; he is also called Царь Водяник, Поддонный царь and Заморский царь."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c07",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s2",
      "quote": "Дворец Морского царя, сделанный из хрусталя, серебра, золота и драгоценных камней, помещается неопределённо: то в синем море, то на острове, то в Ильмень-озере. Когда он пляшет и развлекается — на море шторм и поднимается буря.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istana Tsar Laut dari kristal, perak, emas, dan batu permata letaknya tidak tetap: kadang di laut biru, kadang di sebuah pulau, kadang di Danau Ilmen; ketika ia menari dan bersenang-senang, laut bergolak dan badai pun datang.",
        "en": "The Sea Tsar's palace of crystal, silver, gold and precious stones is placed vaguely: now in the blue sea, now on an island, now in Lake Ilmen; when he dances and makes merry, the sea storms and a tempest rises."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c08",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s2",
      "quote": "Он любит повеселиться, для чего утонувших музыкантов созывает к себе во дворец, где музыканты начинают играть, а водяной царь пляшет под их музыку.",
      "locator": "Облик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia suka bersenang-senang, sehingga memanggil para musisi yang tenggelam ke istananya; mereka bermain musik dan raja air itu menari mengikuti musiknya.",
        "en": "He loves merrymaking and summons drowned musicians to his palace, where they play and the water tsar dances to their music."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c09",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s2",
      "quote": "В былинах он сначала создаёт богатство Садко, а потом, когда Садко разбогател, требует его к себе в подводное царство.",
      "locator": "Народный образ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bylina, ia mula-mula membuat Sadko kaya, lalu setelah Sadko kaya ia menuntut Sadko datang ke kerajaan bawah lautnya.",
        "en": "In the bylinas he first creates Sadko's wealth and then, once Sadko has grown rich, demands that he come to his underwater kingdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c10",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s2",
      "quote": "Здесь он также хочет оставить у себя в подводном царстве героя (Ивана-царевича), но полюбившая царевича дочь морского царя, Василиса, убегает вместе с ним и спасает его от погони отца.",
      "locator": "Народный образ",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam dongeng Vasilisa yang Bijak, ia juga ingin menahan sang pahlawan (Ivan-tsarevich) di kerajaan bawah lautnya, tetapi putrinya, Vasilisa, yang jatuh cinta kepada sang pangeran, melarikan diri bersamanya dan menyelamatkannya dari kejaran sang ayah.",
        "en": "In the tale of Vasilisa the Wise he likewise wants to keep the hero (Ivan-tsarevich) in his underwater kingdom, but his daughter Vasilisa, who has fallen in love with the prince, flees with him and saves him from her father's pursuit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sea-tsar-c11",
      "source_id": "sea-tsar-s2",
      "quote": "Эти водяные, с одутловатым брюхом и опухшим лицом, живущие в омутах, особенно около мельниц, известные пьяницы, посещающие кабаки, играющие в кости, ворующие лошадей и коров и топящие людей, — совершенно отличны от былинного морского царя, который живёт в богатых палатах и изображается страстным любителем музыки.",
      "locator": "Народный образ",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para vodyanoy berperut gembung dan berwajah bengkak yang tinggal di lubuk dekat penggilingan, mabuk-mabukan, main dadu, mencuri kuda dan sapi, serta menenggelamkan orang, sama sekali berbeda dengan Tsar Laut bylina yang tinggal di istana mewah dan digambarkan sebagai pencinta musik.",
        "en": "The vodyanoy, with bloated bellies and swollen faces, living in pools especially near mills, drinking, playing dice, stealing horses and cows and drowning people, are wholly different from the Sea Tsar of the bylinas, who lives in rich halls and is portrayed as a passionate lover of music."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Asal-usul gambaran Tsar Laut",
        "en": "Origin of the Sea Tsar's image"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Tsar Laut adalah tokoh dongeng dan bylina rakyat Rusia.",
            "en": "The Sea Tsar is a character of Russian folktales and bylinas."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "sea-tsar-c06"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Vsevolod Miller menduga gambarannya dipengaruhi dewa Finlandia Ahti dan berasal dari kisah para pencerita keliling, bukan kepercayaan pagan Slavia Timur kuno.",
            "en": "Vsevolod Miller suspected his image was influenced by the Finnish god Ahti and came from itinerant storytellers rather than old East Slavic pagan belief."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "sea-tsar-c02",
            "sea-tsar-c03"
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, ru; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."

    }
  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shatan adalah makhluk netral dalam cerita rakyat Belarus yang menjadi lambang kemalasan: mereka berkelana tanpa tujuan dan mengajak manusia ikut membuang waktu.",
    "en": "Shatans are neutral creatures of Belarusian folklore who symbolise idleness: they wander aimlessly and lure people into the same waste of time.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shatans-c01",
      "shatans-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shatan adalah makhluk berpenampilan aneh dalam cerita rakyat Belarus. Mereka tidak jahat dan tidak pula baik, melainkan menjadi lambang kemalasan. Nikolai Nikiforovsky mencatat mereka dalam kumpulan kisah rakyat tentang kekuatan najis di Belarus wilayah Vitebsk.",
      "en": "Shatans are odd-looking creatures of Belarusian folklore. They are neither evil nor good, but serve as symbols of idleness. Nikolai Nikiforovsky recorded them in his collection of folk tales about unclean spirits in Vitebsk Belarus.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shatans-c01",
        "shatans-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sepanjang hari shatan berkeliaran tanpa tujuan di jalan, ladang, dan hutan, dan mereka menular: orang yang mereka dekati terbujuk ikut berkelana sia-sia. Mereka tidak bisa bergaul, bahkan dengan sesamanya, mengisap tembakau berbau busuk, saling menggeram, dan tidak saling menolong saat kesulitan.",
      "en": "All day long shatans roam aimlessly along roads, fields and forests, and their habit is catching: people they latch on to are drawn into the same pointless wandering. They cannot get along even with one another, smoke foul tobacco, growl at each other and do not help one another in trouble.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shatans-c02",
        "shatans-c06",
        "shatans-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Shatan penakut: ketika diganggu makhluk jahat seperti penyihir, mereka bersembunyi atau lari. Saat lelah, mereka menganyam sepatu kulit kayu dan membuat tongkat, tetapi semuanya cepat aus dan patah karena pengembaraan mereka yang tak berujung.",
      "en": "Shatans are cowardly: when teased by evil beings such as the witch, they hide or run away. When tired they weave bast shoes and make sticks, but these soon wear out and break from their endless roaming.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shatans-c03",
        "shatans-c04",
        "shatans-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],
