  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "shatans-c01",
      "source_id": "shatans-s1",
      "quote": "are odd-looking creatures of Belarusian folklore. Shatans are neutral characters and act as symbols of idleness and laziness.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shatan adalah makhluk berpenampilan aneh dalam cerita rakyat Belarus; mereka tokoh netral yang menjadi lambang kemalasan.",
        "en": "Shatans are odd-looking creatures of Belarusian folklore; they are neutral characters who act as symbols of idleness and laziness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c02",
      "source_id": "shatans-s1",
      "quote": "Shatans are very annoying for other people and dispose them to the same way of useless wandering. Shatans cannot communicate with anyone in a normal way, even with each other, and in case of trouble they don't rescue and help one another.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shatan sangat mengganggu orang lain dan mendorong mereka ikut berkelana tanpa guna; mereka tidak bisa berkomunikasi dengan normal, bahkan dengan sesamanya, dan tidak saling menolong saat kesulitan.",
        "en": "Shatans are very annoying to others and lead them into the same useless wandering; they cannot communicate normally with anyone, even each other, and do not help one another in trouble."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c03",
      "source_id": "shatans-s1",
      "quote": "In Belarusian folklore it is said that angry folkloric creatures (for example, the witch) tease shatans. As shatans are also known as cowardly creatures which hide or run away from offenders.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Belarus disebutkan bahwa makhluk-makhluk pemarah (misalnya penyihir) mengganggu shatan, dan karena shatan dikenal penakut, mereka bersembunyi atau lari dari pengganggunya.",
        "en": "Belarusian folklore says that angry folk creatures (the witch, for example) tease shatans, and since shatans are cowardly they hide or run from their tormentors."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c04",
      "source_id": "shatans-s1",
      "quote": "When shatans are tired of wandering around, they sometimes spin bast shoes. Bast shoes are quickly worn away because of the shatans' useless wandering.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika lelah berkelana, shatan kadang menganyam sepatu kulit kayu (lapti), yang cepat aus karena pengembaraan mereka yang sia-sia.",
        "en": "When tired of wandering, shatans sometimes weave bast shoes, which quickly wear out from their useless wandering."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c05",
      "source_id": "shatans-s2",
      "quote": "белорусские мифологические персонажи, описанные Николаем Никифоровским в работе «Нечистики. Свод простонародных в Витебской Белоруссии сказаний о нечистой силе».",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shatan adalah tokoh mitologi Belarus yang dideskripsikan oleh Nikolai Nikiforovsky dalam karyanya tentang kisah-kisah rakyat mengenai kekuatan najis di Belarus wilayah Vitebsk.",
        "en": "Shatans are Belarusian mythological characters described by Nikolai Nikiforovsky in his work on the folk tales of unclean spirits in Vitebsk Belarus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c06",
      "source_id": "shatans-s2",
      "quote": "Шатаны могут целыми днями бесцельно бродить по дорогам, полям, лесам, не делая ничего полезного.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shatan bisa seharian berkeliaran tanpa tujuan di jalan, ladang, dan hutan tanpa melakukan apa pun yang berguna.",
        "en": "Shatans can roam aimlessly all day along roads, fields and forests without doing anything useful."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c07",
      "source_id": "shatans-s2",
      "quote": "В «Мифологическом словаре» слово «шатана» приведено как синоним сатаны.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kamus mitologi tertentu, kata «шатана» dicantumkan sebagai sinonim untuk setan.",
        "en": "In a certain mythological dictionary, the word «шатана» is given as a synonym for Satan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c08",
      "source_id": "shatans-s3",
      "quote": "Яны паляць смуродны тытунь і гыркаюць адзін на аднаго. Ні ўсмешкі, ні добрага слова ад іх не дачакаешся.",
      "locator": "Апісанне",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka mengisap tembakau berbau busuk dan saling menggeram; tak ada senyum atau kata baik yang bisa ditunggu dari mereka.",
        "en": "They smoke foul-smelling tobacco and growl at one another; you will wait in vain for a smile or a kind word from them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shatans-c09",
      "source_id": "shatans-s3",
      "quote": "Калі Шатаны стамляюцца ад пастаяннага хаджэнні, яны часам плятуць лапці і робяць сабе кіі. Лапці хутка сціраюцца, а кіі ламаюцца ад пастаянна бескарыснага бадзяння.",
      "locator": "Лад жыцця",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat lelah berjalan terus, shatan kadang menganyam lapti dan membuat tongkat, tetapi lapti cepat aus dan tongkat patah karena pengembaraan mereka yang sia-sia.",
        "en": "When tired of constant walking, shatans sometimes weave bast shoes and make themselves sticks, but the shoes soon wear out and the sticks break from their endless useless roaming."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shtojzovalle adalah makhluk kecil yang sangat elok dalam mitologi Albania, tak kasatmata, tinggal di hutan dan padang rumput, serta gemar bernyanyi dan menari.",
    "en": "The shtojzovalle is a small, extraordinarily beautiful being of Albanian mythology, invisible to humans, living in forests and pastures and fond of song and dance.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shtojzovalle-c01",
      "shtojzovalle-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Albania, shtojzovalle adalah makhluk kecil lelaki dan perempuan yang sangat elok dan berkekuatan gaib. Mereka tinggal di hutan dan padang rumput, menghabiskan waktu dengan bernyanyi dan menari, dapat terbang, dan pada malam hari memintal benang kehidupan manusia. Manusia jarang bisa melihat mereka, kecuali dengan mengangkat selubung tak kasatmata di depan matanya.",
      "en": "In Albanian mythology the shtojzovalles are small male and female beings of extraordinary beauty and supernatural power. They live in forests and pastures, spend their time singing and dancing, can fly, and at night spin the thread of human existence. Humans rarely see them, except by lifting an invisible veil before their eyes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shtojzovalle-c01",
        "shtojzovalle-c03",
        "shtojzovalle-c09",
        "shtojzovalle-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hubungan mereka dengan manusia beragam. Kadang terjadi perkawinan, tetapi lelaki hanya bisa mempertahankan istri shtojzovalle jika memberinya pakaiannya sendiri. Rumah yang dibangun di tanah mereka akan dihantui bunyi rantai besi berat, dan orang yang menginjak shtojzovalle bisa dimangsa.",
      "en": "Their dealings with humans vary. Marriages sometimes happen, but a man can keep a shtojzovalle wife only by giving her his own clothes. A house built on their land will be haunted by the rattling of heavy iron chains, and someone who steps on a shtojzovalle may be devoured.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shtojzovalle-c04",
        "shtojzovalle-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya, hasil perpaduan kepercayaan pagan dan Kristen, berarti \"semoga Tuhan menambah tarian mereka\". Di kalangan Katolik Albania utara dan Malësia, shtojzovalle dianggap malaikat yang tetap netral ketika Malaikat Agung Mikael melawan Setan. Mereka menangis menyesali kenetralan itu, dan air mata mereka mematikan manusia; dari sinilah ungkapan i ka rënë pika, \"setetes jatuh padanya\", untuk kematian seseorang.",
      "en": "Their name, a product of pagan and Christian syncretism, means \"may God give increase to their dance\". Among the Catholics of northern Albania and Malësia, the shtojzovalles are held to be angels who stayed neutral when Archangel Michael fought Satan. They weep in repentance for that neutrality, and their tears are deadly to humans; hence the phrase i ka rënë pika, \"a drop fell upon him\", for someone's death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shtojzovalle-c02",
        "shtojzovalle-c06",
        "shtojzovalle-c07",
        "shtojzovalle-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "en": "Belief in the shtojzovalles' tears gave rise to the Albanian phrase i ka rënë pika, used as a figure of speech for death.",

    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c01",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "In Albanian mythology, the shtojzovalle, also known as shtojzorreshta, shtozote is a small creature with supernatural powers and of extraordinary beauty. They are invisible woodland creatures, which can be seen by humans only on rare occasions and are fond of song and dance.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Albania, shtojzovalle (juga disebut shtojzorreshta atau shtozote) adalah makhluk kecil berkekuatan gaib dan sangat elok; mereka makhluk hutan yang tak kasatmata, jarang terlihat manusia, dan gemar bernyanyi serta menari.",
        "en": "In Albanian mythology the shtojzovalle (also shtojzorreshta or shtozote) is a small creature with supernatural powers and extraordinary beauty; they are invisible woodland creatures, rarely seen by humans, fond of song and dance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c02",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "The term shtojzovalle derives from the Albanian words shtoj (English: add), zot (English: god, deity) and valle (English: dance), with the term meaning in English \"may God give increase to their dance\"",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilah shtojzovalle berasal dari kata Albania shtoj (menambah), zot (Tuhan, dewa), dan valle (tarian), sehingga berarti \"semoga Tuhan menambah tarian mereka\".",
        "en": "The term shtojzovalle derives from the Albanian shtoj (add), zot (god, deity) and valle (dance), meaning \"may God give increase to their dance\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c03",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "They live in forests and pastures and spend their time singing and dancing. They have the ability to fly and at night they spin the thread of human existence. They are invisible to humans and a human can see them only if they lift an invisible veil in front of their eyes.",
      "locator": "Characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka tinggal di hutan dan padang rumput, menghabiskan waktu dengan bernyanyi dan menari, mampu terbang, dan pada malam hari memintal benang kehidupan manusia; manusia hanya bisa melihat mereka jika mengangkat selubung tak kasatmata di depan matanya.",
        "en": "They live in forests and pastures, spend their time singing and dancing, can fly, and at night spin the thread of human existence; a human can see them only by lifting an invisible veil before their eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c04",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "Occasionally humans and shtojzovalles are married, but a human can keep a shtojzovalle maiden only if he gives her his clothes to wear.",
      "locator": "Characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang manusia dan shtojzovalle menikah, tetapi seorang lelaki hanya dapat mempertahankan gadis shtojzovalle jika ia memberikan pakaiannya untuk dikenakan sang gadis.",
        "en": "Humans and shtojzovalles occasionally marry, but a man can keep a shtojzovalle maiden only if he gives her his clothes to wear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c05",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "If a human builds their house on lands inhabited by shtojzovalles, the house will be haunted by shtojzovalles shaking heavy iron chains. When people go for a walk and step on a shtojzovalle they may get devoured by it.",
      "locator": "Characteristics",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika manusia membangun rumah di tanah yang dihuni shtojzovalle, rumah itu akan dihantui shtojzovalle yang mengguncang rantai besi berat; orang yang menginjak shtojzovalle saat berjalan bisa dimangsa olehnya.",
        "en": "If a human builds a house on land inhabited by shtojzovalles, it will be haunted by shtojzovalles shaking heavy iron chains; people who step on a shtojzovalle while walking may be devoured by it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c06",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "Albanian Christians and especially the Catholics of northern Albania and Malësia have attributed several Christian characteristics to the shtojzovalles. According to these traditions when Archangel Michael fought against Satan the angels who remained neutral became the shtojzovalles.",
      "locator": "Religious syncretism",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Umat Kristen Albania, terutama umat Katolik di Albania utara dan Malësia, memberi shtojzovalle beberapa ciri Kristen: menurut tradisi ini, ketika Malaikat Agung Mikael melawan Setan, para malaikat yang tetap netral menjadi shtojzovalle.",
        "en": "Albanian Christians, especially the Catholics of northern Albania and Malësia, gave the shtojzovalles Christian features: in these traditions, when Archangel Michael fought Satan, the angels who stayed neutral became the shtojzovalles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shtojzovalle-c07",
      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "The shtojzovalles weep in repentance for their neutrality and if their tears fall on a human he will die. The Albanian phrase i ka rënë pika (English: \"a drop fell upon him\"), metaphorically means that someone died and the phrase is derived from this belief.",
      "locator": "Religious syncretism",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shtojzovalle menangis menyesali kenetralan mereka, dan jika air mata mereka jatuh ke manusia, orang itu akan mati; ungkapan Albania i ka rënë pika (\"setetes jatuh padanya\"), kiasan untuk kematian seseorang, berasal dari kepercayaan ini.",
        "en": "The shtojzovalles weep in repentance for their neutrality, and a human on whom their tears fall will die; the Albanian phrase i ka rënë pika (\"a drop fell upon him\"), a figure of speech for someone's death, comes from this belief."
      }
    },
    {

      "source_id": "shtojzovalle-s1",
      "quote": "The name shtojzovalle is a product of Pagan and Christian religious syncretism. The alternative term shtojzorreshta means \"may God give increase to their rows\" in English.",
      "locator": "Etymology",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama shtojzovalle merupakan hasil sinkretisme agama pagan dan Kristen; istilah lain, shtojzorreshta, berarti \"semoga Tuhan menambah barisan mereka\".",
        "en": "The name shtojzovalle is a product of pagan and Christian religious syncretism; the alternative term shtojzorreshta means \"may God give increase to their rows\"."

    },

        "en": "The Spanish article calls it a small dancing and singing creature of both sexes, of great beauty and supernatural powers, from Albanian mythology, living in mountains and dense forests."

      "en": "Szélatya or Szélkirály is a wind god known in Turkic and Hungarian tradition; in Old Turkic he is Çel Ata, the \"Wind Father\", and the Onoghurs also worshipped him.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c01",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s1",
      "quote": "is the Turkic god or deity of wind.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Szélatya (juga Szélkirály; dalam bahasa Turk Kuno Çel Ede atau Çel Ata, \"Bapa Angin\") adalah dewa angin bangsa Turk.",
        "en": "Szélatya (also Szélkirály; Old Turkic Çel Ede or Çel Ata, \"Wind Father\") is the Turkic god of wind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c02",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s1",
      "quote": "Azerbaijani: Yel ata / Yel baba",
      "locator": "Names",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Azerbaijan namanya Yel ata atau Yel baba.",
        "en": "In Azerbaijani his name is Yel ata or Yel baba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c03",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s1",
      "quote": "Szelatya has long hair, silver weapons, and thunder horses. He is said to appear as a youthful and handsome man in Hungarian mythology, but as an elderly man in Turkic mythology. He has a jungle or sylvan named Silver Forest.",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Szelatya berambut panjang, bersenjata perak, dan memiliki kuda guntur; dalam mitologi Hungaria ia tampil sebagai pemuda tampan, tetapi dalam mitologi Turk sebagai lelaki tua; ia memiliki hutan bernama Hutan Perak.",
        "en": "Szelatya has long hair, silver weapons and thunder horses; in Hungarian mythology he appears as a handsome young man, but in Turkic mythology as an old man; he has a forest called the Silver Forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c04",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s1",
      "quote": "His father is Arany Atyacska (Golden Father) and his mother's name is Hajnal Anyacska (Dawn Mother). However, some accounts mention that he is the son of Kayra, the primordial god.",
      "locator": "Family",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ayahnya Arany Atyacska (Bapa Emas) dan ibunya Hajnal Anyacska (Ibu Fajar), tetapi beberapa kisah menyebutnya putra Kayra, dewa purba.",
        "en": "His father is Arany Atyacska (Golden Father) and his mother Hajnal Anyacska (Dawn Mother), though some accounts make him the son of Kayra, the primordial god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c05",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s1",
      "quote": "His female counterpart is Szélanya.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Padanan perempuannya adalah Szélanya.",
        "en": "His female counterpart is Szélanya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c06",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s2",
      "quote": "је у мађарској митологији бог и божанство ветра.",
      "locator": "увод",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Serbia menyebut Szélatya dan Szélkirály sebagai dewa angin dalam mitologi Hungaria (мађарској митологији).",
        "en": "The Serbian article calls Szélatya and Szélkirály the god of wind in Hungarian mythology (мађарској митологији)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c07",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s1",
      "quote": "The Onoghurs also worshipped him.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bangsa Onoghur juga memujanya.",
        "en": "The Onoghurs also worshipped him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c08",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s3",
      "quote": "Yel Ata - Türk, Altay, Tatar ve Macar mitolojilerinde rüzgâr tanrısı. Cel (Çel, Şil, Cil) Ata da denir.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Turki menyebut Yel Ata sebagai dewa angin dalam mitologi Turk, Altai, Tatar, dan Hungaria, yang juga disebut Cel (Çel, Şil, Cil) Ata.",
        "en": "The Turkish article calls Yel Ata the wind god in Turkic, Altai, Tatar and Hungarian mythologies, also called Cel (Çel, Şil, Cil) Ata."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c09",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s3",
      "quote": "Dünyadaki tüm fırtınaların, rüzgârların, kasırgaların, hortumların, tayfunların yöneticisi olan tanrıdır. Harman savurmak isteyen köylüler Yel Ata'yı yardıma çağırırlar.",
      "locator": "Özellikleri",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yel Ata adalah dewa penguasa semua badai, angin, puting beliung, dan taifun di dunia; para petani yang hendak menampi hasil panen memanggilnya untuk membantu.",
        "en": "Yel Ata is the god ruling all the world's storms, winds, whirlwinds, tornadoes and typhoons; villagers who want to winnow their threshing call on Yel Ata for help."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c10",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s3",
      "quote": "\"Yel Ata, gel atına saman götür...\" diye bağırırlar, o da atına saman götürebilmek için gelip harmanı savurur.",
      "locator": "Özellikleri",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka berseru \"Yel Ata, gel atına saman götür...\" (\"Yel Ata, datanglah, bawakan jerami untuk kudamu\"), dan ia datang menampi panen agar bisa membawa jerami untuk kudanya.",
        "en": "They cry \"Yel Ata, gel atına saman götür...\" (\"Yel Ata, come, take straw for your horse\"), and he comes and winnows the threshing so he can carry straw for his horse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "szelatya-c11",
      "source_id": "szelatya-s3",
      "quote": "(Yel/Yil/İl/Zel/Cel) kökünden türemiştir. Esinti anlamı vardır.",
      "locator": "Etimoloji",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini diturunkan dari akar Yel/Yil/İl/Zel/Cel, yang berarti hembusan angin.",
        "en": "The word derives from the root Yel/Yil/İl/Zel/Cel, meaning a breeze."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga dari Utara atau Katak Utara (Põhja konn) adalah monster raksasa mirip katak dalam cerita rakyat Estonia yang memorakporandakan negeri sampai dikalahkan seorang pemuda dengan cincin ajaib.",
    "en": "The Dragon of the North or Northern Frog (Põhja konn) is a giant frog-like monster of Estonian lore that ravages the land until a young man defeats it with a magic ring.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "the-dragon-of-the-north-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga dari Utara, atau Katak Utara, adalah monster raksasa mirip katak dalam cerita rakyat Estonia. Ia datang dari Utara, menghancurkan wilayah-wilayah luas dan memangsa manusia serta hewan. Tubuhnya tertutup sisik yang lebih kuat dari batu dan besi, dan kedua mata besarnya bersinar siang dan malam seperti lilin.",
      "en": "The Dragon of the North, or Northern Frog, is a giant frog-like monster of Estonian lore. It came out of the North, laying waste whole tracts of country and devouring men and beasts. Its body was covered with scales stronger than stone and iron, and its two great eyes shone day and night like candles.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c01",
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c02",
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c03",
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisahnya dipopulerkan Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald dalam kumpulan dongeng tahun 1866 dengan judul \"Põhja Konn\". Seorang pemuda memperoleh cincin meterai dan memakainya untuk mengalahkan binatang itu, setelah seorang raja menawarkan putri dan separuh kerajaannya kepada siapa pun yang bisa membunuhnya. Lewat terjemahan Jerman, kisah ini masuk ke The Yellow Fairy Book (1894) karya Andrew Lang dan Leonora Blanche Lang.",
      "en": "The tale was popularised by Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald in his 1866 collection under the title \"Põhja Konn\". A young man obtains a signet ring and uses it to defeat the beast, after a king offers his daughter and half his kingdom to whoever can kill it. Through a German translation the story entered The Yellow Fairy Book (1894) by Andrew Lang and Leonora Blanche Lang.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c01",
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c05",
        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c06",

        "the-dragon-of-the-north-c12",

      ]

    {
      "id": "Risto Järv menggolongkan kisah ini sebagai varian lokal kisah pembunuh naga (ATU 300) dengan katak menggantikan naga, dan menurut August Annist, Kreutzwald sendiri yang membuat naganya mirip katak saat menyesuaikan kisah itu dengan konteks Estonia. Karya modern justru sering menjadikan katak itu pelindung, misalnya novel Andrus Kivirähk dan patung karya Navitrolla.",
      "en": "Risto Järv classes the tale as a local variant of the dragon-slayer story (ATU 300) with the frog replacing the dragon, and according to August Annist it was Kreutzwald who made the dragon frog-like when adapting the story to the Estonian setting.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c01",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "is a legendary frog-like creature in Estonian lore. The story was popularized by Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald in the collection Eesti rahva ennemuistsed jutud or Old Tales of the Estonian People (1866).",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga dari Utara atau Katak Utara adalah makhluk legenda mirip katak dalam cerita rakyat Estonia; kisahnya dipopulerkan Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald dalam kumpulan Eesti rahva ennemuistsed jutud (Old Tales of the Estonian People, 1866).",
        "en": "The Dragon of the North or Northern Frog is a legendary frog-like creature of Estonian lore; the story was popularised by Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald in the collection Eesti rahva ennemuistsed jutud (Old Tales of the Estonian People, 1866)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c02",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "The frog is described as a massive, dragon-like monster that wreaks havoc on the land until it is defeated.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Katak itu digambarkan sebagai monster raksasa mirip naga yang memorakporandakan negeri sampai akhirnya dikalahkan.",
        "en": "The frog is described as a massive, dragon-like monster that wreaks havoc on the land until it is defeated."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c03",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "there lived a terrible monster, who came out of the North, and laid waste whole tracts of country, devouring both men and beasts",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah itu, sesosok monster mengerikan datang dari Utara, menghancurkan wilayah-wilayah luas dan memangsa manusia serta hewan.",
        "en": "In the tale, a terrible monster came out of the North, laying waste whole tracts of country and devouring both men and beasts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c04",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "\"The frog's body was completely covered with scales, which were stronger than stone and iron, so that nothing could harm it. Its two large eyes shone night and day like the brightest candles...\"",
      "locator": "Description",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tubuh katak itu tertutup sisik yang lebih kuat dari batu dan besi sehingga tak ada yang bisa melukainya, dan kedua mata besarnya bersinar siang dan malam seperti lilin paling terang.",
        "en": "The frog's body was covered with scales stronger than stone and iron, so nothing could harm it, and its two large eyes shone night and day like the brightest candles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c05",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "The frog appeared in \"Põhja Konn\" (literally \"The Northern Frog\") by Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald",
      "locator": "Origins",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Katak ini muncul dalam kisah \"Põhja Konn\" (harfiah \"Katak Utara\") karya Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald.",
        "en": "The frog appeared in \"Põhja Konn\" (literally \"The Northern Frog\") by Friedrich Reinhold Kreutzwald."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c06",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "In it, Kreutzwald tells the story of a young man who acquires and uses a signet ring to defeat a monstrous beast.",
      "locator": "Origins",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di dalamnya Kreutzwald menceritakan seorang pemuda yang memperoleh dan memakai sebuah cincin meterai untuk mengalahkan binatang raksasa itu.",
        "en": "In it Kreutzwald tells of a young man who obtains and uses a signet ring to defeat the monstrous beast."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c07",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "It also gained international recognition when included in Andrew Lang and Leonora Blanche Lang's The Yellow Fairy Book (1894) under the title \"The Dragon of the North.\"",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah ini dikenal internasional setelah dimuat dalam The Yellow Fairy Book (1894) karya Andrew Lang dan Leonora Blanche Lang dengan judul \"The Dragon of the North\".",
        "en": "The story became internationally known when included in Andrew Lang and Leonora Blanche Lang's The Yellow Fairy Book (1894) as \"The Dragon of the North\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c08",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "Estonian scholar Risto Järv classifies the Northern Frog as a variant of a dragon-slayer (ATU 300) using the Aarne–Thompson–Uther Index, with the frog replacing the dragon and functioning as an oicotype",
      "locator": "Analysis",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarjana Estonia Risto Järv menggolongkan Katak Utara sebagai varian kisah pembunuh naga (ATU 300) dalam Indeks Aarne–Thompson–Uther, dengan katak menggantikan naga sebagai oikotipe (versi lokal).",
        "en": "The Estonian scholar Risto Järv classes the Northern Frog as a variant of the dragon-slayer tale (ATU 300) in the Aarne–Thompson–Uther Index, with the frog replacing the dragon as an oicotype."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c09",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "According to August Annist, in adapting a dragonslayer style of story to the Estonian context, Kreutzwald made the dragon frog-like.",
      "locator": "Analysis",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut August Annist, ketika menyesuaikan kisah pembunuh naga dengan konteks Estonia, Kreutzwald membuat naganya mirip katak.",
        "en": "According to August Annist, in adapting a dragon-slayer story to the Estonian setting, Kreutzwald made the dragon frog-like."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c10",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "In the 2007 novel, The Man Who Spoke Snakish, author Andrus Kivirähk depicts the Northern Frog as a giant, dormant protector of the Estonians against Christian crusaders.",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam novel 2007 The Man Who Spoke Snakish, Andrus Kivirähk menggambarkan Katak Utara sebagai pelindung raksasa bangsa Estonia yang tertidur, melawan tentara salib Kristen.",
        "en": "In the 2007 novel The Man Who Spoke Snakish, Andrus Kivirähk depicts the Northern Frog as a giant, dormant protector of the Estonians against Christian crusaders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c11",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s1",
      "quote": "In 2025, the artist Navitrolla installed a statue in Võru at Tamula Beach called the Tamula konn that explicitly draws on the legend of the Northern Frog. Navitrolla positions the frog as a protector.",
      "locator": "In popular culture",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 2025 seniman Navitrolla memasang patung Tamula konn di Pantai Tamula, Võru, yang secara terang mengambil legenda Katak Utara dan menempatkan katak itu sebagai pelindung.",
        "en": "In 2025 the artist Navitrolla installed the Tamula konn statue at Tamula Beach in Võru, explicitly drawing on the Northern Frog legend and presenting the frog as a protector."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c12",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s2",
      "quote": "Isinama ito ni Andrew Lang sa The Yellow Fairy Book; inilista niya ang kaniyang pinagmulan bilang \"Der Norlands Drache\" mula sa Ehstnische Märchen, na salin sa Aleman ng gawa ni Kreutzwald, ni F. Löwe.",
      "locator": "panimula",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Andrew Lang memasukkannya ke The Yellow Fairy Book dan mencantumkan sumbernya \"Der Norlands Drache\" dari Ehstnische Märchen, terjemahan Jerman karya Kreutzwald oleh F. Löwe.",
        "en": "Andrew Lang included it in The Yellow Fairy Book, citing as his source \"Der Norlands Drache\" from Ehstnische Märchen, F. Löwe's German translation of Kreutzwald's work."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-c13",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-of-the-north-s2",
      "quote": "Pumunta siya sa kaharian kung saan inalok ng isang hari ang kaniyang anak na babae at kalahati ng kaniyang kaharian sa sinumang makapatay ng dragon",
      "locator": "Buod",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sang pemuda pergi ke kerajaan tempat seorang raja menawarkan putrinya dan separuh kerajaannya kepada siapa pun yang bisa membunuh sang naga.",
        "en": "The youth went to the kingdom where a king offered his daughter and half his kingdom to whoever could kill the dragon."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Slavia, arwah najis adalah orang yang mati secara tidak wajar, tidak menemukan kedamaian, dan dipercaya kembali ke dunia orang hidup sebagai makhluk jahat.",
    "en": "In Slavic folklore the unclean dead are people who died an unnatural death, found no peace, and were believed to return to the world of the living as malevolent beings.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c01",
      "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Slavia, arwah najis adalah orang yang mati secara tidak wajar atau hidup dengan buruk, sehingga jiwanya tidak bisa pergi ke alam baka dan mengembara di bumi. Mereka dipercaya kembali ke dunia orang hidup sebagai makhluk jahat. Yang biasanya digolongkan ke dalamnya adalah korban kekerasan, orang yang bunuh diri, mati karena mabuk atau tenggelam, anak yang belum dibaptis, dukun, dan penyihir.",
      "en": "In Slavic belief the unclean dead are people who died an unnatural death or lived a bad life, so that their souls cannot pass into the afterlife and wander the earth instead. They were believed to return to the world of the living as malevolent beings. They typically included victims of violence, suicides, those who died of drink or by drowning, unbaptised children, sorcerers and witches.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c01",
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c03",
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c05",
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c13"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Istilah ilmiah zalozhnye pokoiniki diperkenalkan etnografer Dmitry Zelenin pada awal abad ke-20, karena tradisi-tradisi Slavia tidak punya satu nama bersama untuk mereka. Zelenin mengaitkan kata itu dengan cara penguburannya: jasad diletakkan telungkup dan kuburnya ditutup batu serta ranting. Arwah najis tidak dimakamkan di pemakaman gereja, melainkan di persimpangan jalan, batas ladang, hutan, rawa, atau jurang, karena bumi dipercaya tidak mau menerima mereka.",
      "en": "The scholarly term zalozhnye pokoiniki was introduced by the ethnographer Dmitry Zelenin in the early 20th century, since the Slavic traditions had no single shared name for them. Zelenin linked the word to the manner of burial: the body was laid face down and the grave sealed with stones and branches. The unclean dead were not buried in church cemeteries but at crossroads, field boundaries, forests, swamps or ravines, because the earth was believed to refuse them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c02",
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c04",
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c06",
        "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c14"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Arwah najis dipercaya mendatangkan bencana. Di Kegubernuran Simbirsk, saat kemarau orang menggali jasad pemabuk dan membuangnya ke rawa agar hujan turun, dan legenda Baturka yang rakus menuntut persembahan dari orang yang lewat agar mereka dan ternaknya tidak jatuh sakit. Di Belarus orang yang melewati tempat kematian karena kekerasan melemparkan batu, ranting, jerami, atau tanah ke sana, dan di tempat lain biji poppy yang diberkati ditaruh di kubur agar si mati tidak mendatangi orang hidup.",
      "en": "The unclean dead were believed to bring misfortune. In Simbirsk Governorate, in a drought, people dug up a drunkard's body and threw it into a swamp to bring rain, and the legend of greedy Baturka demanded offerings from passers-by lest they and their livestock fall ill. In Belarus people passing a place of violent death threw stones, branches, straw or earth onto it, and elsewhere blessed poppy seed was placed in the grave so the dead would not visit the living.",

      "id": "Mengapa cara seseorang meninggal begitu penting dalam kepercayaan tentang arwah?",
      "en": "Why does the manner of a person's death matter so much in beliefs about the dead?"
    }

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The unclean dead, according to Slavic folklore, are those who died an unnatural death and did not find peace after death. They were believed to return to the world of the living and continue their existence on earth as malevolent mythical beings.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Slavia, arwah najis adalah orang yang mati secara tidak wajar dan tidak menemukan kedamaian setelah mati; mereka dipercaya kembali ke dunia orang hidup dan melanjutkan keberadaan di bumi sebagai makhluk mitos yang jahat.",
        "en": "According to Slavic folklore, the unclean dead are those who died an unnatural death and found no peace afterwards; they were believed to return to the world of the living and go on existing on earth as malevolent mythical beings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In Russian ethnography, the term zalozhnye pokoiniki (Russian: заложные покойники ) is also used",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam etnografi Rusia dipakai pula istilah zalozhnye pokoiniki (заложные покойники), yang diperkenalkan ke dunia ilmiah pada awal abad ke-20 oleh etnografer Dmitry Zelenin.",
        "en": "Russian ethnography also uses the term zalozhnye pokoiniki (заложные покойники), introduced into scholarship in the early 20th century by the ethnographer Dmitry Zelenin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "It was believed that the soul of a so-called \"restless\" (Russian: неупокоенный ) dead could not pass on to the afterlife and therefore wandered the earth. According to Slavic folklore, such dead could turn into malevolent spirits.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Diyakini bahwa jiwa orang mati yang \"tak tenang\" tidak bisa pergi ke alam baka sehingga mengembara di bumi, dan orang mati semacam itu bisa berubah menjadi roh jahat.",
        "en": "It was believed that the soul of a \"restless\" dead person could not pass into the afterlife and so wandered the earth, and that such dead could turn into malevolent spirits."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Rather, they were buried outside the territory of a church, in places such as at crossroads, field boundaries, forests, swamps, or ravines, since it was believed that they were \"cursed by their parents and the earth would not receive them.\"",
      "locator": "Burial",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dikuburkan di luar lingkungan gereja, misalnya di persimpangan jalan, batas ladang, hutan, rawa, atau jurang, karena dipercaya \"dikutuk orang tua mereka dan bumi tidak mau menerima mereka\".",
        "en": "They were buried outside church grounds, at places such as crossroads, field boundaries, forests, swamps or ravines, because they were believed to be \"cursed by their parents, and the earth would not receive them\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "\"Zalozhny dead\" were typically considered to be those who died violently, suicides, drunken deaths, drowning victims, unbaptized children, sorcerers, and witches.",
      "locator": "\"Zalozhny dead\" in Russian ethnography",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yang biasanya dianggap \"zalozhny\" adalah orang yang mati karena kekerasan, bunuh diri, mati karena mabuk, mati tenggelam, anak yang belum dibaptis, dukun, dan penyihir.",
        "en": "\"Zalozhny dead\" were typically those who died violently, suicides, those who died of drink, the drowned, unbaptised children, sorcerers and witches."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "He linked the origin of the word “zalozhny” to the burial method itself: the body was placed face down in the coffin, and the grave was sealed with stones and branches",
      "locator": "\"Zalozhny dead\" in Russian ethnography",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zelenin mengaitkan asal kata \"zalozhny\" dengan cara penguburannya: jasad diletakkan telungkup di dalam peti dan kuburnya ditutup dengan batu dan ranting.",
        "en": "Zelenin linked the origin of the word \"zalozhny\" to the burial method: the body was laid face down in the coffin and the grave sealed with stones and branches."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "по славянским верованиям, умершие неестественной смертью люди, не получившие после смерти успокоения.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rusia menyebut заложные покойники sebagai orang yang menurut kepercayaan Slavia mati tidak wajar dan tidak mendapat ketenangan setelah mati.",
        "en": "The Russian article describes заложные покойники as people who, in Slavic belief, died an unnatural death and found no rest after death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "В Симбирской губернии существовало поверье, что во время засухи «надо непременно найти опойца, которого не принимает земля, поэтому его надо вырыть из земли и кинуть в болото, чтобы пошёл дождь».",
      "locator": "Народные поверья",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Kegubernuran Simbirsk ada kepercayaan bahwa saat kemarau orang harus menemukan pemabuk yang tidak diterima bumi, menggalinya, dan membuangnya ke rawa agar hujan turun.",
        "en": "In Simbirsk Governorate it was believed that during a drought one had to find a drunkard whom the earth would not accept, dig him up and throw him into a swamp so that rain would come."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c09",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Известна, например, легенда о Батурке, который по поверью был очень жадным, поэтому, чтобы утолить его алчность, проходящие мимо люди должны были сделать подношение, иначе он мог нагнать на них и их скот болезнь.",
      "locator": "Народные поверья",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada legenda tentang Baturka yang dipercaya sangat rakus, sehingga orang yang lewat harus meninggalkan persembahan; kalau tidak, ia bisa mendatangkan penyakit kepada mereka dan ternaknya.",
        "en": "There is a legend of Baturka, believed to be very greedy, so that passers-by had to leave an offering or he might bring disease on them and their livestock."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c10",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "В Беларуси места насильственной смерти считались нечистыми, и проходящие мимо люди на такие места бросали камни, ветки деревьев, клок соломы или горсть земли («иначе умерший будет за ними долго гнаться»).",
      "locator": "Народные поверья",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Belarus tempat kematian karena kekerasan dianggap najis, dan orang yang lewat melemparkan batu, ranting, seikat jerami, atau segenggam tanah ke tempat itu agar si mati tidak mengejar mereka.",
        "en": "In Belarus places of violent death were considered unclean, and passers-by threw stones, branches, a tuft of straw or a handful of earth onto them, \"or else the dead man will chase them for a long time\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c11",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Дмитрий Зеленин ставил знак равенства между «заложными» покойниками и русалками.",
      "locator": "Мифологические персонажи",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dmitry Zelenin menyamakan arwah \"zalozhny\" dengan rusalka.",
        "en": "Dmitry Zelenin equated the \"zalozhny\" dead with rusalkas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c12",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Упырь — «заложный» покойник, чаще всего умерший колдун.",
      "locator": "Мифологические персонажи",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Upyr adalah arwah \"zalozhny\", paling sering seorang dukun yang telah mati.",
        "en": "The upyr is a \"zalozhny\" dead person, most often a dead sorcerer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c13",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "збірна назва істот, що за слов'янськими, зокрема українськими, народними повір'ями були людьми, котрі померли неприродною смертю, або які жили поганим, негідним життям, і тому їхні душі не можуть піти до потойбічного світу.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Ukraina menyebut Заложні мерці sebagai nama kolektif makhluk yang menurut kepercayaan rakyat Slavia, termasuk Ukraina, dulunya manusia yang mati tidak wajar atau hidup buruk dan tak layak, sehingga jiwanya tidak bisa pergi ke alam baka.",
        "en": "The Ukrainian article calls Заложні мерці a collective name for beings who, in Slavic and especially Ukrainian folk belief, were people who died an unnatural death or lived a bad, unworthy life, so their souls cannot pass to the other world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c14",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "В багатьох місцях вірили, що земля не приймає заложних мерців, їхні тіла не розкладаються і земля сама виштовхує їх на поверхню.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di banyak tempat dipercaya bahwa bumi tidak menerima arwah najis: jasad mereka tidak membusuk dan bumi sendiri mendorong mereka ke permukaan.",
        "en": "In many places it was believed that the earth does not accept the unclean dead: their bodies do not decay and the earth itself pushes them to the surface."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-c15",
      "source_id": "unclean-dead-in-slavic-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Аби позбавити таких покійників здатності відвідувати живих людей, в їхні могили клали вузлик свяченого маку-видюку. Власні ж хати обсипали свяченим маком або льоном.",
      "locator": "Поводження із заложними мерцями",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Agar arwah semacam itu tidak bisa mendatangi orang hidup, ke dalam kuburnya diletakkan bungkusan biji poppy yang telah diberkati, dan rumah sendiri ditaburi poppy atau rami yang diberkati.",
        "en": "To stop such dead from visiting the living, a bundle of blessed poppy seed was placed in their graves, and people sprinkled their own houses with blessed poppy or flax."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Utqaçı adalah roh langit dalam syamanisme masyarakat Altai, utusan dewa tertinggi Ülgen yang menyambut dukun di langit dan membawa kurbannya kepada Ülgen.",
    "en": "Utqaçı is a heavenly spirit of Altai shamanism, messenger of the supreme god Ülgen, who meets shamans in the sky and carries their sacrifices to Ülgen.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Utqaçı adalah tokoh dalam syamanisme masyarakat asli Altai. Ia termasuk roh langit atau roh dunia atas, berperan sebagai utusan roh tertinggi Ülgen, dan hanya tinggal di langit tanpa pernah turun ke bumi. Sumber berbahasa Azerbaijan dan Turki menyebutnya dewa kurban dalam mitologi Turk dan Altai.",
      "en": "Utqaçı is a figure of the shamanism of the indigenous Altai peoples. He is one of the heavenly spirits of the upper world, serves as messenger of the supreme spirit Ülgen, and lives only in the sky, never coming down to earth. Azerbaijani and Turkish sources call him the god of sacrifice in Turkic and Altai mythology.",

      "source_id": "utqac-s1",
      "quote": "Уткучы — персонаж шаманского культа коренных народов Алтая.",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Utqaçı (Уткучы) adalah tokoh dalam kultus perdukunan (syamanisme) masyarakat asli Altai.",
        "en": "Utqaçı (Уткучы) is a figure of the shamanic cult of the indigenous peoples of the Altai."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c02",
      "source_id": "utqac-s1",
      "quote": "Уткучы считается одним из небесных духов (духов «верхнего» мира). Исполняет роль посланника верховного духа Ульгеня. Живёт только на небе, на землю не спускается.",
      "locator": "Место в «иерархии духов»",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Utqaçı dianggap salah satu roh langit (roh dunia \"atas\"); ia berperan sebagai utusan roh tertinggi Ülgen, hanya tinggal di langit dan tidak turun ke bumi.",
        "en": "Utqaçı is considered one of the heavenly spirits (spirits of the \"upper\" world); he serves as messenger of the supreme spirit Ülgen, lives only in the sky and never comes down to earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c03",
      "source_id": "utqac-s1",
      "quote": "Встреча происходила на так называемом «пятом слое неба», который располагался у Полярной звезды",
      "locator": "Роль в общении с шаманами",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertemuan Utqaçı dengan dukun terjadi di \"lapisan langit kelima\" dekat Bintang Kutub.",
        "en": "Utqaçı met the shaman on the so-called \"fifth layer of the sky\", located by the Pole Star."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c04",
      "source_id": "utqac-s1",
      "quote": "Уткучы слушал шамана, забирал у него жертвенное животное и отводил его к Ульгеню, передавая тому слова камлавшего. Возвратившись, Уткучы озвучивал волю верховного божества и давал шаману гусей, которые доставляли его на землю.",
      "locator": "Роль в общении с шаманами",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Utqaçı mendengarkan dukun, mengambil hewan kurbannya dan membawanya ke Ülgen beserta pesan sang dukun; setelah kembali, ia menyampaikan kehendak dewa tertinggi dan memberi dukun angsa-angsa yang membawanya pulang ke bumi.",
        "en": "Utqaçı listened to the shaman, took his sacrificial animal and led it to Ülgen with the shaman's words; returning, he announced the supreme god's will and gave the shaman geese that carried him back to earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c05",
      "source_id": "utqac-s2",
      "quote": "Utqaçı Xan - türk və altay mifologiyasında qurban ilahı. Utkuçı da deyilir. Qurbanları Ülgenə çatdırar.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Azerbaijan menyebut Utqaçı Xan sebagai dewa kurban dalam mitologi Turk dan Altai, juga disebut Utkuçı, yang menyampaikan kurban kepada Ülgen.",
        "en": "The Azerbaijani article calls Utqaçı Xan the god of sacrifice in Turkic and Altai mythology, also called Utkuçı, who delivers sacrifices to Ülgen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c06",
      "source_id": "utqac-s1",
      "quote": "Буквальный перевод имени этого духа — «приветливо встречающий».",
      "locator": "Место в «иерархии духов»",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama roh ini secara harfiah berarti \"yang menyambut dengan ramah\".",
        "en": "The spirit's name literally means \"the one who greets warmly\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c07",
      "source_id": "utqac-s3",
      "quote": "Utkaçı Han - Türk ve Altay mitolojisinde Kurban Tanrısı.",
      "locator": "giriş",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Turki menyebut Utkaçı Han sebagai Dewa Kurban dalam mitologi Turk dan Altai.",
        "en": "The Turkish article calls Utkaçı Han the God of Sacrifice in Turkic and Altai mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "utqac-c08",
      "source_id": "utqac-s3",
      "quote": "(Ut) kökünden türemiştir. Güleryüzlü, kazanan demektir.",
      "locator": "Etimoloji",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama ini diturunkan dari akar Ut dan berarti \"yang ramah\" atau \"yang menang\".",
        "en": "The name derives from the root Ut and means \"cheerful\" or \"winning\"."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Vadleány, \"gadis liar\", adalah makhluk hutan dalam cerita rakyat Hungaria yang berambut sangat panjang dan telanjang, yang menggoda pengembara dan mencuri kekuatan mereka.",
    "en": "The vadleány, the \"wild girl\", is a forest being of Hungarian folklore with very long hair and no clothing, who seduces wanderers and steals their strength.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "vadleany-c01",
      "vadleany-c02",
      "vadleany-c04"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vadleány adalah makhluk roh dari cerita rakyat Eropa Tengah, khususnya Hungaria, yang namanya berarti \"gadis liar\". Ia mirip nimfa atau driad: tinggal di hutan, berambut sangat panjang, berkuku panjang, dan telanjang. Ia menggoda para pengembara dan mencuri kekuatan mereka, dan pepohonan berdesir ketika ia hadir.",
      "en": "The vadleány is a spirit being of Central European, particularly Hungarian, folklore whose name means \"wild girl\". She resembles nymphs or dryads: she lives in the forest, has very long hair and long nails, and goes naked. She seduces wanderers and steals their strength, and the trees rustle when she is present.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vadleany-c01",
        "vadleany-c02",
        "vadleany-c03",
        "vadleany-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Orang Hungaria di Lápos menggambarkannya berwajah cantik tetapi bertubuh penuh bulu sampai ke tanah. Bila dibuat marah, ia menimbulkan angin yang mematahkan pohon dan memorakporandakan pondok gembala. Di Domokos dipercaya ia bisa ditangkap dengan meletakkan sebelah sepatu bot di jalurnya: karena penasaran ia memasukkan kedua kakinya dan tidak bisa lari. Di Mezőség dan Székelyföld ia disebut gadis hutan, nona penyanyi, atau gadis riang.",
      "en": "The Hungarians of Lápos described her as beautiful of face but covered in hair down to the ground. When angered she raises a wind that breaks trees and scatters the shepherd's hut. In Domokos it was believed she could be caught by setting a single boot on her path: out of curiosity she put both feet in and could not run. In Mezőség and Székelyföld she was called the forest girl, the singing maiden or the merry girl.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vadleany-c05",
        "vadleany-c06",
        "vadleany-c07",
        "vadleany-c08"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c01",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s1",
      "quote": "A Vadleány is a spiritual being from Central European (Hungarian) folklore. Similar to nymphs or dryads, Vadleány have extremely long hair, long fingernails, and are completely nude.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vadleány adalah makhluk roh dari cerita rakyat Eropa Tengah (Hungaria); mirip nimfa atau driad, ia berambut sangat panjang, berkuku panjang, dan sepenuhnya telanjang.",
        "en": "The Vadleány is a spirit being of Central European (Hungarian) folklore; like nymphs or dryads, she has extremely long hair and long fingernails and is completely nude."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c02",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Encyclopedia of Fairies in World Folklore and Mythology, they seduce wanderers and steal their strength. The name translates to \"wild girl\".",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Encyclopedia of Fairies in World Folklore and Mythology, vadleány menggoda para pengembara dan mencuri kekuatan mereka; namanya berarti \"gadis liar\".",
        "en": "According to the Encyclopedia of Fairies in World Folklore and Mythology, vadleány seduce wanderers and steal their strength; the name means \"wild girl\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c03",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s1",
      "quote": "When one is present, the trees make a rustling sound.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika seorang vadleány hadir, pepohonan berdesir.",
        "en": "When a vadleány is present, the trees rustle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c04",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s2",
      "quote": "A vadleány egy természetfeletti lény a közép-európai (azon belül a magyar) folklórban. Erdei lény, aki elcsábítja az utazókat, és ellopja az erejüket.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Hungaria menyebut vadleány sebagai makhluk gaib dalam folklor Eropa Tengah, khususnya Hungaria: makhluk hutan yang menggoda para pelancong dan mencuri kekuatan mereka.",
        "en": "The Hungarian article calls the vadleány a supernatural being of Central European, in particular Hungarian, folklore: a forest being who seduces travellers and steals their strength."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c05",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s2",
      "quote": "A vadleányt ismerték a láposi magyarok is: meztelenül jár, az arca szép, a testét azonban földig érő szőr fedi.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Hungaria di Lápos (láposi magyarok) juga mengenal vadleány: ia berjalan telanjang, wajahnya cantik, tetapi tubuhnya tertutup bulu yang menjuntai sampai ke tanah.",
        "en": "The Hungarians of Lápos (láposi magyarok) also knew the vadleány: she goes naked (meztelenül jár), her face is beautiful, but her body is covered with hair reaching to the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c06",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s2",
      "quote": "Ha felbosszantják, olyan szelet csinál, hogy fákat tör ki, széthányja a pásztor kalibáját. A domokosiak szerint meg lehetett fogni: amerre járni szokott, fél pár csizmát kellett kitenni.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jika dibuat marah, ia menimbulkan angin yang mematahkan pohon dan memorakporandakan pondok gembala; menurut orang Domokos, ia bisa ditangkap dengan meletakkan sebelah sepatu bot di jalur yang biasa dilaluinya.",
        "en": "If angered she raises a wind that breaks trees and scatters the shepherd's hut; according to the people of Domokos she could be caught by setting out one boot of a pair where she usually walked."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c07",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s2",
      "quote": "A képzetet ismerték a mezőségi és székelyföldi magyarok is: erdei leánynak, éneklő kisasszonynak, víg leánynak emlegették.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Hungaria di Mezőség dan Székelyföld juga mengenal gambaran ini dan menyebutnya erdei leány (gadis hutan), éneklő kisasszony (nona penyanyi), dan víg leány (gadis riang).",
        "en": "The Hungarians of Mezőség and Székelyföld also knew this figure, calling her erdei leány (forest girl), éneklő kisasszony (singing maiden) and víg leány (merry girl)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vadleany-c08",
      "source_id": "vadleany-s2",
      "quote": "Kíváncsiságában felpróbálta, de mindkét lábát beledugta, így aztán nem futhatott el.",
      "locator": "bevezető",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena penasaran ia mencobanya, tetapi memasukkan kedua kakinya sehingga tidak bisa lari.",
        "en": "Out of curiosity she tried it on but put both feet in, and so could not run away."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Vâlvă adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Rumania yang berjalan di puncak bukit pada malam hari; ada yang baik (Vâlve Putih) dan ada yang jahat (Vâlve Hitam), dan banyak jenisnya menjaga tempat atau harta tertentu.",
    "en": "The vâlvă is a female spirit of Romanian folklore who walks the hilltops at night; some are good (White Vâlve) and some evil (Black Vâlve), and many kinds guard particular places or treasures.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "valva-c01",
      "valva-c04",
      "valva-c05",
      "valva-c08"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vâlvă adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Rumania yang dipercaya berjalan di puncak bukit pada malam hari. Ada Vâlve Albe yang baik dan Vâlve Negre yang jahat. Mereka sering tampil sebagai perempuan, tetapi bisa juga muncul sebagai bayangan atau kucing hitam, dan dapat melindungi desa dari badai, hujan es, dan angin topan.",
      "en": "The vâlvă is a female spirit of Romanian folklore believed to walk the hilltops at night. There are the good Vâlve Albe and the evil Vâlve Negre. They often appear as women but may also come as shadows or black cats, and they can protect a village from storm, hail and hurricane.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valva-c01",
        "valva-c02",
        "valva-c04",
        "valva-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Vâlve terbagi dalam banyak jenis menurut apa yang mereka jaga. Vâlva Apei melindungi mata air dan pancuran, Vâlva Comorilor menjaga harta dan bisa menunjukkan tempat harta terkubur, dan Vâlva Ciumei menguasai wabah sampar serta penyakit lain. Vâlva Băilor melindungi tambang; kepergiannya menandakan bijih akan habis.",
      "en": "The vâlve fall into many kinds according to what they guard. Vâlva Apei protects water sources and fountains, Vâlva Comorilor guards treasures and can reveal where they are buried, and Vâlva Ciumei controls plague and other diseases. Vâlva Băilor protects mines; her departure signals that the ore is running out.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valva-c03",
        "valva-c05",
        "valva-c07",
        "valva-c08",
        "valva-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ada pula pandangan lain: vâlve adalah anak-anak yang dikandung pada hari raya besar dan lahir dengan semacam topi kecil berwarna merah di kepala. Para solomonar pun konon termasuk Vâlve, orang-orang berbeda yang memiliki kekuatan istimewa.",
      "en": "Another view holds that vâlve are children conceived on great feast days and born with a small red cap on the head. The solomonari, too, are said to be Vâlve, different people with special powers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "valva-c10",
        "valva-c11"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "valva-c01",
      "source_id": "valva-s1",
      "quote": "Vâlvă (plural vâlve) is a female spirit mentioned in the Romanian folklore. The Vâlve are believed to walk over the hilltops at night, and are subdivided into Vâlve Albe (\"White Vâlve\"), who are considered beneficial, and Vâlve Negre (\"Black Vâlve\" or \"Dark Vâlve\"), who are considered evil.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlvă (jamak vâlve) adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Rumania yang dipercaya berjalan di puncak-puncak bukit pada malam hari; mereka terbagi menjadi Vâlve Albe (\"Vâlve Putih\") yang baik dan Vâlve Negre (\"Vâlve Hitam\") yang jahat.",
        "en": "Vâlvă (plural vâlve) is a female spirit of Romanian folklore believed to walk over the hilltops at night; they are divided into the beneficial Vâlve Albe (\"White Vâlve\") and the evil Vâlve Negre (\"Black Vâlve\")."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c02",
      "source_id": "valva-s1",
      "quote": "They may also appear under various guises, such as shadows or black cats. They also have the ability to shapeshift.",
      "locator": "lead",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka bisa muncul dalam berbagai wujud, seperti bayangan atau kucing hitam, dan mampu berubah wujud.",
        "en": "They may appear in various guises, such as shadows or black cats, and can shapeshift."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c03",
      "source_id": "valva-s1",
      "quote": "Vâlva Băilor (\"of the mines\"), defender and protector of mines and tunnels, whose departure means that the deposit is coming to an end",
      "locator": "Types",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlva Băilor (\"dari tambang\") adalah pembela dan pelindung tambang dan terowongan; kepergiannya berarti endapan bijih akan habis. Legenda tentangnya berkembang di daerah tambang logam tradisional di Rumania.",
        "en": "Vâlva Băilor (\"of the mines\") is the defender and protector of mines and tunnels, whose departure means the deposit is running out. Legends of her flourish in traditional metal-mining areas of Romania."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c04",
      "source_id": "valva-s2",
      "quote": "Вылва (рум. Vâlva) — в румынской мифологии женский дух, бродящий ночью по вершинам холмов. Вылвы могут быть как добрыми (vâlve albe, белые), так и злыми (vâlve negre, чёрные).",
      "locator": "преамбула",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rusia menyebut Вылва sebagai roh perempuan dalam mitologi Rumania yang berkeliaran di puncak bukit pada malam hari, dan bisa baik (vâlve albe, putih) atau jahat (vâlve negre, hitam).",
        "en": "The Russian article calls the Вылва a female spirit of Romanian mythology who roams the hilltops at night and may be good (vâlve albe, white) or evil (vâlve negre, black)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c05",
      "source_id": "valva-s2",
      "quote": "Вылва воды (Vâlva Apei) — защитница водных источников и фонтанов.",
      "locator": "Виды",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlva Apei adalah pelindung mata air dan pancuran.",
        "en": "Vâlva Apei is the protector of water sources and fountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c06",
      "source_id": "valva-s2",
      "quote": "Вылвы могут принимать разный облик. Часто они выглядят как женщины, иногда просто как тени или чёрные кошки. Вылвы могут защищать село от бури, града, урагана.",
      "locator": "Облик",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlve bisa mengambil berbagai wujud: sering seperti perempuan, kadang hanya bayangan atau kucing hitam; mereka bisa melindungi desa dari badai, hujan es, dan angin topan.",
        "en": "Vâlve can take various forms: often women, sometimes mere shadows or black cats; they can protect a village from storm, hail and hurricane."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c07",
      "source_id": "valva-s2",
      "quote": "Вылва чумы (Vâlva Ciumei) — управляет чумой и другими болезнями.",
      "locator": "Виды",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlva Ciumei menguasai wabah sampar dan penyakit lain.",
        "en": "Vâlva Ciumei controls plague and other diseases."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c08",
      "source_id": "valva-s2",
      "quote": "Вылва сокровищ (Vâlva Comorilor) — защитница сокровищ, которая также может указать место, где зарыт клад.",
      "locator": "Виды",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlva Comorilor adalah pelindung harta yang juga bisa menunjukkan tempat harta karun dikubur.",
        "en": "Vâlva Comorilor is the protector of treasures who can also show where treasure is buried."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c09",
      "source_id": "valva-s2",
      "quote": "Вылва руды (Vâlva Băilor) — защитница шахт; когда она покидает копь, считается, что руда заканчивается.",
      "locator": "Виды",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vâlva Băilor adalah pelindung tambang; jika ia meninggalkan tambang, bijihnya dianggap akan habis.",
        "en": "Vâlva Băilor is the protector of mines; when she leaves a mine, the ore is thought to be running out."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c10",
      "source_id": "valva-s3",
      "quote": "Se spune că ar fi copiii care sunt concepuți la zile mari, de sărbătoare. În zonele rurale se spune că acești copii se nasc cu o tichiuță sau cu o gogoașă pe cap, roșie",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Rumania menyebut bahwa vâlve konon adalah anak-anak yang dikandung pada hari raya besar, dan di pedesaan dikatakan anak-anak ini lahir dengan semacam topi kecil atau benjolan merah di kepala.",
        "en": "The Romanian article says vâlve are said to be children conceived on great feast days, and in rural areas such children are said to be born with a small red cap or swelling on the head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "valva-c11",
      "source_id": "valva-s3",
      "quote": "Se spune despre solomonari că sunt Vâlve, că sunt oameni diferiți, având puteri deosebite.",
      "locator": "introducere",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon para solomonar adalah Vâlve, orang-orang yang berbeda dan memiliki kekuatan istimewa.",
        "en": "The solomonari are said to be Vâlve, people who are different and have special powers."
      }
    }
  ],
